6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
But getting inflation back to target in the face of an oil price
spike and high administered prices will not be easy and involves
some tough trade-offs. Administered prices tend to be less
responsive to changes in economic conditions. As a result,
interest rate hikes are likely to have a bigger impact on parts of

the economy that are not the main drivers of inflation. Tighter
policy will also weigh on the labour market and near-term
growth, while increasing households’ debt-servicing costs.
This is an uncomfortable but necessary trade-off. Looking
through, or not responding to, these sorts of large and persistent
cost increases would still leave households facing significant
increases in overall living costs. In the long run, it would be
more damaging for households and the economy if generalised

inflation were to become embedded in price setting behaviour.
The Kiwi has lost its wings
Adding to near-term inflation risks has been the drop in the NZ
dollar. On a trade-weighted basis, the Kiwi has fallen around
4% since the start of September, and it’s begun the December
quarter around 4% below the level the RBNZ assumed in its
September policy statement.
The slide in the Kiwi is mainly due to the strength of the US
dollar, with NZD/USD down nearly 5% since the start of

September to sit just below 0.56 at the time of writing. Moves
against other currencies have been more modest. For example,
NZD/AUD is down 2% over the same period.
Technically, NZD/USD seems oversold. In the short term,
trends in the US dollar will dominate, including any response
to the US labour market report due on Friday evening. Any
turnaround in the USD would likely see NZD/USD trade back
towards the top of the 0.5580–0.5680 range that is governing

the pair for the time being.
NZD/AUD will continue to be driven by changing views on
interest rate differentials between the RBNZ and RBA. This
week, NZD/AUD corrected higher from around 0.8070 to as high
as 0.8110 as RBA cash rate expectations were repriced following
the RBA Board meeting and Australian CPI. In the short term, the
bias for NZD/AUD could be lower, given our view that the RBNZ
will pause at its late-October meeting and that the market is

under-pricing the risk of an RBA rate hike in November.
Next week’s QSBO, due on Tuesday, will be the key event
risk, with the inflation and growth gauges closely watched for
signs that might prompt the RBNZ to pause or hike. Markets
had previously priced in a high chance of a hike at the RBNZ’s
October meeting, but in recent days appear to be reassessing
that view.  Pricing for a 25bp hike in New Zealand could be
pared back towards a 50:50 call. The 0.8030–0.8150 range

is likely to contain the pair in the immediate period ahead.
Our medium-term view is for NZD/AUD to rise as the RBNZ
increases the OCR to 4% by the second half of 2027. We’ll
update our views on the NZD and other currencies in our next
Market Outlook, out Wednesday 14 October.
Fiscal update
The big uncertainty on the domestic horizon is, of course,
November’s general election, and at this stage the polling
looks tight. Neither the established left nor right bloc has a

clear majority.
Ahead of the election, the Treasury has released its Pre-election
Economic and Fiscal Update (PREFU), updating the Budget
2026 (BEFU) fiscal forecasts to reflect the latest economic and
fiscal information and a revised economic outlook. This allows
the public to assess the state of the Government’s finances. It
also provides other parties with a baseline against which to
calibrate their spending and taxation policies.
The PREFU revealed a stronger starting point for the

Government’s finances than had been expected in the Budget.
• A deficit of $8.6bn is estimated for 2025/26, down from the
Budget estimate of $11.9bn.
• Similarly, the forecast deficit for 2026/27 has been revised
down significantly to $6.8bn from $11.4bn.
• Thereafter, the OBEGALx deficit is expected to continue
declining gradually, moving to a surplus of $4bn in 2028/29.
This broadly follows the Budget profile, but is stronger
across the forecast period.

• Net core Crown debt is forecast to peak at 43.9% of GDP in
2027/28, below the 46.1% peak forecast in the BEFU. It’s then
forecast to decline to 39.5% of GDP by the end of the forecast
period in 2030/31, in line with the Government’s goal of
reducing debt below 40% of GDP over the medium term.
Given the improved outlook, NZDM announced a $4bn
reduction in the 2026/27 bond programme to $30bn and a
$15bn reduction in the forecast programme across the whole

period through to 2029/30.
The medium-term forecasts in the PREFU should be treated
with caution. The economic forecasts underpinning the
PREFU were finalised on 21 August, when both oil prices
and, especially, bond yields were materially lower than they
are today. Had the forecasts been finalised more recently, the
fiscal projections presented today would have been weaker,
at least in the near term. Indeed, the Treasury is upfront in
acknowledging that, while the risks are two-sided, the balance

is skewed to the downside.
Aside from the obvious uncertainty surrounding the economic
outlook because of unpredictable developments in the Middle
East, the approaching General Election, and the possibility of
a change in the composition of the Government, means the
assumptions underpinning the PREFU forecasts could quickly
become invalidated.

AUS: Oct Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment (index)
Oct 6, Last: 84.4
Consumer sentiment declined 5.2% to 84.4 in September,
taking the index back towards the deeply pessimistic levels seen
earlier in the year. Rising fuel prices and fears of an imminent

interest rate rise drove the move with a continued weakening in
housing markets likely adding to unease in some quarters.
Developments over the last month would have added to these
concerns with: the RBA raising the cash rate by 25bps to 4.6%
during the survey week; average pump prices pushing above
$2.36/litre nationally and no end in sight to global supply
disruptions;  and more weak updates on the housing market.
NZ: Q2 NZIER Survey of Business Opinion (index)

Oct 6, General business confidence last: 12.3
Business conditions picked up modestly in the June quarter as
concerns about the war in the Middle East eased. However,
cost and pricing gauges remained firm. The September quarter
survey will be closely watched ahead of the RBNZ’s 28
October meeting. Recent weeks have seen signs that activity
is firming. However, fuel prices have also risen sharply, and we
(along with the RBNZ) will be watching for signs of a broader

lift in inflation pressures. The timing of survey responses
within the sample period will also warrant scrutiny, given that
oil prices rose late in the quarter.

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm sâu trong tuần qua, 57.87 điểm tương ứng -3.12%, với thanh khoản 
suy giảm rõ rệt. Đồ gia dụng, Hàng xa xỉ & dệt may, Bảo hiểm là các nhóm ngành có mức 
giảm rõ nét nhất trong tuần. Khối ngoại tiếp tục động thái bán ròng so với xu hướng của 
tuần trước với giá trị -1,634 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã VPB, CTG và VCB; đồng thời, tự 
doanh duy trì bán ròng ở mức -317 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại bán ròng 3,421 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua nhưng 
đang duy trì vị thế mua lũy kế 1,992 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 1,432 hợp đồng nhưng 
đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng 199 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam có tuần điều chỉnh đầu tiên sau 3 tuần tăng liên tiếp khi VN-Index đánh 
mất mốc tâm lý 1.800 điểm và lùi về đóng cửa quanh 1.795 điểm. Áp lực chốt lời sau nhịp 
hồi tích cực trong tháng 8, cộng hưởng với rủi ro căng thẳng Trung Đông leo thang là 2 
nguyên nhân chính khiến áp lực bán tăng mạnh. Nhà đầu tư nước ngoài bán ròng 4/5 
phiên trong tuần qua cho thấy dòng vốn ngoại vẫn dè dặt ngay trước thời điểm nâng hạng 
— làm dấy lên lo ngại kịch bản "mua kỳ vọng, bán thực tế" quanh ngày 21/9. Nhìn chung, 
tâm lý thị trường chung có sự chuyển biến thận trọng trước các sự kiện quan trọng sắp 
diễn ra: diễn biến giá dầu và địa chính trị Trung Đông, cuộc họp chính sách của Fed giữa 
tháng 9, và thời điểm Việt Nam chính thức được FTSE nâng hạng lên thị trường mới nổi 
thứ cấp vào 21/9.

CTCP Tập đoàn Gemadept (GMD)

03/09/2026   
Chuyên viên cao cấp Nguyễn Ngọc Anh 
(+84) 24-7303-5333 anhntn@kbsec.com.vn

Kết quả kinh doanh 2Q2026 tiếp tục 
tăng trưởng tích cực

Sản lượng hàng qua cảng GMD tiếp 
tục duy trì đà tăng trưởng bền vững

Giá dịch vụ kỳ vọng duy trì đà tăng 
ổn định từ nay đến 2030

Khuyến ngh ị MUA – Giá m ục tiêu 
92,500 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Doanh thu thuần của GMD 2Q2026 đạt 1,762 tỷ VND (+18% yoy). Tổng sản lượng qua 
hệ thống cảng GMD đ ạt 1.35 tri ệu Teu (+7% yoy). LNTT 2Q2026 của GMD ghi nh ận 
1,550 tỷ VND, tăng m ạnh 129% yoy do ghi nh ận khoản lãi tài chính 60 9 tỷ VND t ừ 
chuyển nhượng cổ phần công ty liên kết. Lũy kế 6 tháng đầu năm 2026, doanh thu và 
LNTT của GMD lần lượt hoàn thành 49%/81% doanh thu và LNTT kế hoạch 2026.

Sản lượng hàng qua h ệ thống cảng GMD k ỳ vọng đạt tăng trưởng 11.6% yoy trong 
2026 và CAGR 9%/năm giai đo ạn 2026 –2030. 2H2026, Nam Đình Vũ đư ợc k ỳ vọng 
phục hồi nhờ có thêm 2 tuyến dịch vụ mới, trong khi Gemalink ti ếp tục tăng trưởng 
nhờ bổ sung tuyến mới và ngu ồn hàng ổn định từ hãng tàu đ ối tác. Triển vọng dài 
hạn được củng cố bởi dư đ ịa công su ất tại Nam Đình Vũ 3 và Gemalink 2  trong bối 
cảnh nguồn cung Cái Mép đến 2030 dự kiến vẫn thấp hơn nhu cầu.

Giá dịch vụ tại các cảng GMD kỳ vọng duy trì đà tăng trung bình 5 –7%/năm tại phía 
Bắc và 7-10%/năm tại phía Nam giai đoạn 2026–2030, được hỗ trợ bởi (1) dư địa tăng 
giá còn lớn so với các nước khác trong khu vực và (2) khung trần giá dịch vụ tại cảng 
nước sâu vừa được Bộ Xây dựng nâng 10% từ 01/02/2026.

Chúng tôi khuy ến ngh ị MUA đ ối với cổ phiếu GMD, giá m ục tiêu là 92,500 VND/cổ 
phiếu, cao hơn 16.4% so với mức giá đóng cửa ngày 28/08/2026.

KQKD 6M2026 tăng trưởng khả quan 
so với cùng kỳ

HRC là động lực chính thúc đ ẩy tăng 
trưởng sản lượng tiêu thụ thép

Kỳ vọng giá bán thép có xu hư ớng 
được điều chỉnh tăng sau Q3/2026

Tiếp tục nâng cao kh ả năng sản xuất 
để nắm bắt các cơ hội mới

Khuyến ngh ị MUA, giá m ục tiêu 
32,150 VND/cổ phiếu 
 HPG ghi nhận doanh thu và LNST (loại trừ thu nhập từ chuyển nhượng dự án) 6M2026 
đạt lần lượt 108,060/11,538 t ỷ VND, tăng 47%/52% svck. Đ ộng lực tăng trư ởng chủ 
yếu đến từ: (1) tiến độ giải ngân đầu tư công đư ợc đẩy mạnh, (2) các d ự án hạ tầng 
quy mô l ớn bước vào giai đo ạn thi công, (3) các bi ện pháp thu ế quan giúp thu h ẹp 
chênh lệch giá giữa HRC nội địa và nhập khẩu.

Chúng tôi dự báo tổng sản lượng tiêu thụ thép năm 2026/2027 đạt lần lượt 14.2/15.5 
triệu tấn. Lũy kế 6M2026, sản lượng bán hàng c ủa Hòa Phát đ ạt 6.8 tri ệu tấn, trong 
đó s ản lượng HRC đ ạt 3.4 tri ệu tấn tăng 57% svck, nh ờ Dung Qu ất 2 đã v ận hành 
khoảng 92% công suất thiết kế và mảng thép xây dựng tăng 17% svck nhưng các dây 
chuyền hiện tại đã hoạt động hết công suất.

Do xung đ ột chính tr ị và thắt ch ặt ngu ồn cung than c ốc, các doanh nghi ệp trong 
ngành đã điều chỉnh giá bán thép xây dựng và HRC bình quân lần lượt +8%/+12% để 
giảm áp lực lên biên lợi nhuận. KBSV kỳ vọng giá thép tăng trở lại từ Q4/2026 khi các 
doanh nghiệp trong ngành tăng cường nhập hàng để phục vụ nhu cầu xây dựng và 
tiến độ thi công đẩy nhanh vào giai đoạn cuối năm.

Hòa Phát đang triển khai đầu tư các d ự án lớn, trong đó bao g ồm (1) các h ạng mục 
thuộc Khu liên hợp Dung Quất, (2) dự án nhà máy thép ray và thép đặc biệt công suất 
700,000 tấn/năm, và (3) d ự án container. Chúng tôi gi ữ quan điểm tích c ực về triển 
vọng mở rộng danh m ục sản phẩm giá tr ị gia tăng của HPG, song v ẫn cần theo dõi 
thêm tiến độ vận hành, sản lượng thực tế và năng lực cạnh tranh được phân tích dưới 
báo cáo.

KBSV duy trì khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 32,150 VND/cổ phiếu, tương ứng mức 
lợi nhuận kỳ vọng 48.2% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 04/09/2026.

TCT Cảng Hàng không Việt Nam (ACV) 
Tăng trưởng chậm lại trước chu kỳ đầu tư mới 
07/09/2026  
Chuyên viên cao cấp Nguyễn Ngọc Anh 
(+84) 24-7303-5333 anhntn@kbsec.com.vn

KQKD tăng trư ởng chậm l ại trong 
2Q2026

Lượt khách qua cảng hàng không 
Việt Nam duy trì tăng trưởng bền 
vững giai đoạn từ nay đến 2030

Số lượt khách quốc tế/nội địa của 
ACV dự kiến đạt tăng trưởng trung 
bình 6.8/3.3%/năm giai đoạn 2026-
2030

Long Thành 1 đang được đẩy nhanh 
tiến độ để kịp khai thác từ đầu 2027

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP, 
giá mục tiêu 46,600 VND/cổ phiếu

2Q2026, ACV ghi nhận doanh thu đạt 6,324 tỷ VND (-0.3% yoy), chủ yếu do (1) ACV đã 
hoàn thành chuy ển nhượng Cảng hàng không (CHK) Phú Qu ốc cho Sun Group vào 
1/1/2026 và (2) ảnh hưởng từ việc cắt giảm đường bay Trung Đông. Lũy k ế 6 tháng 
đầu năm 2026, ACV ghi nhận doanh thu và LNST công ty mẹ đạt 13,164/5,278 tỷ VND 
(+4%/12% yoy).

Chúng tôi d ự phóng lư ợt khách hàng không Vi ệt Nam tăng trung bình kho ảng 
9%/năm giai đoạn 2026–2030 nhờ nền kinh tế duy trì tăng trưởng cao, các hãng hàng 
không mở rộng đội bay, mạng bay và công suất sân bay được bổ sung liên tục, với đà 
tăng được kỳ vọng cải thiện từ 2H2026 khi áp lực từ xung đột Trung Đông và giá nhiên 
liệu giảm bớt.

Tăng trưởng hành khách quốc tế và nội địa thông qua hệ thống cảng hàng không của 
ACV dự kiến đạt CAGR lần lượt 6.8% và 3.3% giai đoạn 2026–2030, thấp hơn mức tăng 
trưởng chung của toàn thị trường hàng không Việt Nam do một phần tăng trưởng lưu 
lượng hành khách sẽ được hấp thụ bởi các sân bay ngoài hệ thống ACV trong những 
năm tới, bao gồm dự án Cảng hàng không Gia Bình và Phú Quốc.

Dự án Cảng HKQT Long Thành 1 đang đư ợc đẩy nhanh tiến độ dù một số hạng mục 
vẫn còn khối lượng lớn, dự kiến hoàn thành và bắt đầu khai thác thương mại từ đầu 
2027, bổ sung công suất 25 triệu khách/năm cho ACV.

Dựa trên tri ển vọng kinh doanh và k ết quả định giá, chúng tôi khuy ến nghị TRUNG 
LẬP đối với cổ phiếu ACV, giá mục tiêu là 46,600 VND/cổ phiếu, cao hơn 13.7% so với 
giá đóng cửa ngày 04/09/2026.

Tăng trưởng tín dụng được dự báo 
tiếp tục duy trì tích cực

NIM cả năm 2026, được kỳ vọng vẫn 
cải thiện nhẹ so với 2025

Chất lư ợng tài s ản suy gi ảm nh ẹ 
nhưng vẫn trong tầm kiểm soát

Khuyến ngh ị: MUA – Giá m ục tiêu 
26,300 VNĐ/CP  
 Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng tăng trưởng tín dụng 2026 đạt 15%, với động lực thúc đẩy 
chính đến từ dư nợ phân khúc KHDN, đặc biệt là nhóm KHDN lớn và FDI, mặc dù nhu 
cầu đi vay của phân khúc bán l ẻ trong năm dự kiến sẽ chậm hơn do ảnh hưởng của 
mặt bằng lãi suất cao.

NIM có thể bị ảnh hưởng phần nào do tình hình huy động thị trường 1 chưa khả quan 
buộc ngân hàng phải phụ thuộc vào kênh giấy tờ có giá với chi phí cao hơn trong nửa 
cuối năm. Dù vậy, chúng tôi kỳ vọng NIM của ngân hàng vẫn có thể cải thiện nhẹ so 
với cuối năm 2025 nhờ (1) ngân hàng đẩy mạnh cho vay trung dài hạn với lợi suất tốt 
hơn; (2) danh mục cho vay tiếp tục được tái định giá ở mức cao hơn trong các quý tới; 
và (3) ngân hàng có thêm nguồn huy động offshore với chi phí vốn hợp lý.

Chất lượng tài sản trong 2026 dự kiến suy giảm nhẹ, phản ánh môi trường lãi suất 
cho vay tăng cao. Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng tỷ lệ NPL sẽ tăng 13bps so với 2025 và 
tăng dự phóng đối với chi phí trích lập dự phòng so với báo cáo kỳ trước do tác 
động của Nghị định 86 lên hoạt động trích lập của ngân hàng trong nửa đầu năm 
mạnh hơn nhận định trước đó.

Dựa trên triển vọng kinh doanh của ngân hàng cũng như đánh giá các y ếu tố rủi ro, 
chúng tôi tiếp tục duy trì khuyến nghị MUA với cổ phiếu ACB với giá mục tiêu 26,300 
VNĐ/CP - tiềm năng tăng giá 18% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 08/09/2026.

Khuyến ngh ị: MUA – Giá m ục tiêu 
38,750 VNĐ/CP  
 Chúng tôi nâng dự phóng tăng trưởng tín dụng của ngân hàng trong giai đoạn 
2026F-2028F lên khoảng 18-19% nhờ (1) hưởng lợi từ chính sách nới tín dụng đối 
với các dự án hạ tầng trọng điểm, (2) mối quan hệ chiến lược với các nhà thầu lớn, 
và (3) chủ động đa dạng nguồn huy động để đáp ứng nhu cầu tín dụng.

Với luận điểm ngân hàng s ẽ đẩy mạnh cho vay các d ự án hạ tầng thuộc chủ trương 
của Chính phủ và mặt bằng lãi suất đang neo ở mức cao, chúng tôi cho r ằng NIM sẽ 
tiếp tục chịu áp lực trong năm 2026, trước khi cải thiện dần trong các năm tiếp theo 
khi áp lực lãi suất hạ nhiệt và thu nhập lãi từ các khoản vay thuộc chính sách Flexible 
Pricing sẽ được thu hồi.

Chất lượng tài sản dự kiến suy giảm nhẹ, phản ánh môi trường lãi suất neo cao gây 
áp lực lên danh mục bán lẻ của ngân hàng. Chúng tôi nâng dự phóng đối với tỷ lệ 
NPL lên thêm 10bps so với báo cáo kỳ trước và nâng nhẹ chi phí dự phòng với kỳ 
vọng ngân hàng sẽ tiếp tục duy trì bộ đệm an toàn ở mức cao.

Dựa trên triển vọng kinh doanh của ngân hàng cũng như đánh giá các y ếu tố rủi ro, 
chúng tôi ti ếp tục duy trì khuy ến ngh ị MUA đối với cổ phiếu TCB v ới giá m ục tiêu

56,700 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Doanh thu thuần 2Q2026 đạt 5,472 tỷ VND (+10% yoy), trong khi LNST đạt 73 tỷ 
VND, giảm 25% yoy do biên lợi nhuận thu hẹp đến từ (1) tăng chi phí nhiên liệu và 
(2) tăng chi phí vận hành, khấu hao cho các dự án đầu tư mới. Lũy kế 6T2026, 
doanh thu và LNST của VTP lần lượt đạt 10,230/112 tỷ VND, hoàn thành 52%/28% 
doanh thu và LNST kế hoạch của 2026.

Chúng tôi dự phóng sản lượng chuyển phát Việt Nam tăng 22% yoy trong 2026 và duy 
trì CAGR 15% giai đoạn 2026–2030 nhờ nhu cầu giao nhận tiếp tục mở rộng theo đà 
tăng trưởng của thị trường TMĐT . VTP kỳ vọng tiếp tục gia tăng th ị phần lên 23.5% 
năm 2026 và đ ạt 25.5% vào 2030, duy trì t ốc độ tăng trưởng sản lượng cao hơn th ị 
trường.

VTP đang đ ẩy nhanh đ ầu tư các trung tâm logistics t ại Lạng Sơn, Hưng Yên và Đà 
Nẵng, đồng thời mở rộng hoạt động tại Lào, Campuchia và Trung Qu ốc nhằm từng 
bước hoàn thiện mạng lưới logistics xuyên biên gi ới, tạo nền tảng tham gia sâu hơn 
vào chuỗi logistics toàn trình. Chúng tôi d ự phóng doanh thu kho vận và vận tải đạt 
CAGR 22%, trong khi logistics xuyên biên gi ới tăng trung bình 29%/năm giai đo ạn 
2026–2030.

Dựa trên tri ển vọng kinh doanh và k ết quả định giá, chúng tôi đưa ra khuy ến nghị 
TRUNG LẬP đối với cổ phiếu VTP , với giá m ục tiêu 56,700 VND/c ổ phiếu, tương ứng 
mức tăng giá ti ềm năng 12.5%so v ới giá đóng c ửa 50,400 VND/c ổ phiếu ngày 
09/09/2026.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

Nhịp tăng điểm sau kỳ nghỉ lễ đã sớm cho tín hiệu đảo chiều trong tuần qua với sự hình 
thành của một mẫu nến phân phối tiêu cực trên khung tuần. Trên khung ngày, 2 phiên 
giảm mạnh vào đầu và cuối tuần cũng cho thấy áp lực bán mang tính áp đảo với độ rộng 
thị trường hoàn toàn nghiêng về số mã giảm điểm. Với đà phân phối hiện tại, áp lực điều 
chỉnh có thể còn tiếp diễn trong những phiên kế tiếp, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng tăng ngắn 
hạn đang được bảo lưu, VNINDEX sẽ có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại tại các điểm đỡ bên dưới. Ở 
góc nhìn rộng hơn, xu hướng trung hạn có phần trung tính khi chỉ số đang hình thành các 
mẫu hình vận động đi ngang theo tam giác hoặc hình bình hành.

trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua.

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

Joseph Quinlan

Anna Potts, CFA®

MACRO STRATEGY 
MARKET VIEW 
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK 
MARKETS IN REVIEW 
Portfolio Considerations
We view recent volatility as a rolling
correction rather than the end of
the cycle. We favor diversified Equity

exposure, with potential opportunities
extending beyond Growth to Value,
Small- and Mid-caps, Non-U.S. Equities
(especially Emerging Markets), dividend-
focused and thematic strategies.
We favor adjusting portfolios toward
strategic durations given attractive
elevated nominal and real yields, and see
value in high-quality bonds, particularly
longer-dated municipal bonds.
Consider broader portfolio
diversification with potential
opportunities across industrial metals,

energy, Real/Tangible Assets, Hedge
Funds, private markets and global
strategies to capture themes from AI,
reindustrialization, infrastructure growth
and market dispersion.
All data, projections and opinions are as of the date of this report and subject to change.
I
N THIS  ISSUE
Macro Strategy—The  Yen, 10-Year Yield and Iran: Bessent’s Three-Front War and
the Stakes for
Investors: Scott Bessent may carry the title of Treasury secretary, but
these days he looks more like America’s financial field marshal. His battlefield stretches

from T
okyo, where he is trying to engineer a stronger Japan yen, to the U.S. Treasury
market, where he is battling to keep the 10-year yield from breaking  decisively above
5%, and to T
ehran, where he has become the point man in the escalating U.S. campaign
to squeeze Iran financially and economically.
Ho
w Scott Bessent’s three-front war plays out remains to be seen. A decisive victory on
all three fronts is unlikely since corralling currency and bond markets is next to impossible,

while the whims of geopolitics can lay waste to the best laid plans. That said, however,
the U.S. Secretary of the Treasury has signaled that he is ready to take a very  proactive,
pro-growth approach to his job. That’s the good news. The less encouraging news stems
from the inconsistencies in the overarching objectives of the Treasury secretary.
Market View—Beyond AI’s Resource Footprint, Consider its “Handprint”: Behind
ever
y Artificial Intelligence (AI)-generated response is a vast physical network of data

centers, power plants, transmission lines, cooling systems, semiconductor facilities
and critical-mineral supply chains. As adoption accelerates, growing demand for power,
water, advanced hardware and raw materials could strain grids, raise emissions and
intensify pressure
on local resources. Yet the sustainability conversation should extend
beyon
d AI’s footprint to consider whether its environmental benefits can outweigh its
resource demands.
Thought of the Week—The Beauty of September: Call it an eventful first 13 trading

days of September: Brent crude re-eclipsed $100 per barrel, diesel prices surpassed
$6
per gallon for the first time in history, the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield breached 5%, and
the Federal Reserve (Fed) joined the European Central Bank (ECB) and the Bank of Japan
(BoJ) in hiking rates for the
first time since July 2023. Through it all, the S&P 500 has
shed 1.4% since the start of the month. It’s not unexpected that September would be
choppy for U.S. Equities, especially in a midterm election year. The good news is that

this has historically been an attractive entry point. Since 1950, the S&P 500 has never
been
negative in the six or twelve months post-midterm election, boasting average
returns of 13.8% and 15.8%, respectively. Volatility is likely to rise into October before
moderating post-election. A correction amid a strong fundamental economic backdrop
would be a
potential buying opportunity in our view.

### Page 2

2 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The Yen, 10-Year Yield and Iran:  Bessent’s Three-Front War and the Stakes for Investors
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Scott Bessent may carry the title of Treasury Secretary, but these days he looks more like
America’s financial field marshal. His battlefield stretches from Tokyo, where he is trying
to engineer a stronger Japan yen; to the U.S. Treasury market, where he is battling to keep

the 10-year yield from breaking decisively above 5%; and Tehran, where he has become the
point man in the escalating U.S. campaign to squeeze Iran financially and economically.
Make no mistake about it: Secretary Bessent and the Treasury Department are increasingly
an instrument of American economic statecraft, charged not simply with financing the
government and overseeing the financial system, but with managing markets, currencies
and geopolitical adversaries simultaneously. Welcome to the new age of financial warfare.

Front One: Defending the Yen. The first front unfolded over the summer when Secretary
Bessent and the BoJ stealthily intervened in support of the yen, which had fallen to a near
40-year low against the dollar by late July. To arrest the yen’s decline, the U.S. Treasury sold
euros instead of dollars in support of the yen, while pushing the BoJ to make use of the
Fed’s emergency repo facilities. In further support of the yen, the BoJ did what Bessent and

the markets have been pining for, raising its official rate by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%.
Since intervening in the foreign exchange markets, the yen has appreciated by nearly 5%
against the U.S. dollar, alleviating concerns of a disorderly unwind of the yen and giving
Bessent—thus far—an important victory. Defending the yen, of course, isn’t simply about
Japan—it’s about the U.S. and the need for foreign capital inflows.
Japan is one of America’s largest foreign creditors. A persistently weak yen creates

pressure on Tokyo to defend its currency, potentially by selling some of its enormous
holdings of foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys. That prospect didn’t sit well with the
Treasury secretary. At precisely the moment Washington needs foreign capital to finance
its enormous fiscal deficits, the last thing Mr. Bessent wants is one of America’s largest
creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities. Hence, the battle to backstop

Japan’s currency.
Front Two: Defending the 10-Year Bond. Mr. Bessent has had less success on the
second front: the U.S. bond market. While the 10-year Treasury yield slipped slightly
following last week’s decision by the Fed to boost the fed funds rate by 25 bps, the
benchmark interest rate is up roughly 85 bps since the start of the year. Stubbornly high
inflation, elevated oil prices, enormous government borrowing requirements and growing
investor anxiety over America’s fiscal trajectory have all converged and conspired to keep

the 10-year yield closer to 5% than 4%.
Field Marshal Bessent is very aware that the 10-year Treasury is the gravitational center of
global finance—and that mortgage rates, corporate borrowing costs, equity valuations and
the government’s own interest bill all take their cue from it. A sustained move materially
above 5% would tighten financial conditions across the American economy, potentially
threaten the Equity bull market, and pile more pressure on the Republican Party.

To prevent this from happening, the Treasury has expanded its bond-buyback operations,
including purchases of longer-dated government securities, in an effort to improve liquidity
and relieve some pressure on the market, while shifting new government debt issuance
into short-term bills. Yet the bond vigilantes have so far proved harder to intimidate than
currency traders. Even after Treasury announced additional purchases, yields continued
climbing before receiving some relief from the latest hawkish move from the Fed.

Over the medium term, if investors believe Washington is borrowing too much, inflation is
running too hot or the supply of bonds is overwhelming global demand, they will demand a
higher yield to own America’s debt. This battle is far from over.
Front Three: Squeezing Tehran. Then there is Iran, where Mr. Bessent is wielding an
entirely different weapon: America’s control over the plumbing of global finance. In practice
Investment Implications
We expect continued investor

support and preference for real hard
assets, and a bias towards a plethora
of commodities and industrial plays.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
this means the Treasury can make a foreign bank choose between doing business
with Iran or the U.S. It can freeze assets, blacklist companies, restrict transactions and
pressure allies to close financial escape routes.
Under “Operation Economic Outcast,” billed as economic “D-Day,” Treasury has
intensified sanctions against Iranian entities and financial networks, including recent

actions against Iran aviation and additional efforts aimed at banks and organizations
facilitating Tehran’s international transactions. Mr. Bessent has described the objective
in unusually stark terms: to “economically asphyxiate” the Iran regime. This is economic
warfare in its purest form, but whether it works remains to be seen.
“Asphyxiating” an economy takes time and patience—and international cooperation
and coordination. It means closing multiple global loopholes of third-party entities still

willing and able to do business with Iran—look no further than Russian banks. In addition,
squeezing a major oil producer runs the risk of aggravating global oil supplies, keeping
oil prices elevated. Higher energy costs, in turn, feed right back into the U.S. inflation
outlook the Fed is trying to tame and keeps the bond vigilantes on guard and ready to
push back. Not unlike the battle to suppress U.S. bond yields, the battle to bring Iran to
heel is going to be a long one.

How does it all end? How Scott Bessent’s three-front war plays out remains to be seen. A
decisive victory on all three fronts is unlikely since corralling currency and bond markets
is next to impossible, while the whims of geopolitics can lay waste to the best-laid plans.
That said, however, the U.S. Treasury Secretary has signaled that he is ready to take
a very proactive, pro-growth approach to his job. That’s the good news. The less
encouraging news stems from the inconsistencies in the overarching objectives of the

Treasury secretary.
Mr. Bessent can pressure Iran because access to the dollar-based financial system
remains extraordinarily valuable. He can intervene in foreign-exchange markets because
America’s financial resources give Washington enormous clout. And he can attempt to
influence the Treasury market because U.S. government securities remain the foundation
of the global financial system. But therein lies the contradiction.
The U.S. is simultaneously weaponizing the financial system abroad while becoming more

dependent on that same financial system at home. Washington wants the world to fear
losing access to dollars while also asking the world to lend it trillions of dollars. It wants to
use Treasury and dollar dominance as instruments of geopolitical coercion while running
fiscal deficits that require foreigners to continue believing deeply in those very assets.
The more Washington borrows, the more sensitive it becomes to bond-market discipline.
The more aggressively it weaponizes access to the dollar, the greater the incentive for

adversaries to seek alternatives. And the more the Treasury secretary intervenes directly
in markets, the more investors may begin wondering where economic policy ends and
market management begins.

For the moment, Mr. Bessent is fighting on three fronts. He wants a stronger yen, a
lower 10-year Treasury yield and an economically isolated Iran. Success on any one front
would be difficult. Achieving all three simultaneously will require considerable skill—and
perhaps a little luck.

MARKET VIEW
Beyond AI’s Resource Footprint, Consider its “Handprint”
Anna Potts, CFA, Vice President and Investment Strategist
Since the launch of ChatGPT, investors have celebrated AI’s potential while growing
more concerned about its resource footprint: Behind every AI-generated response is
a physical network of data centers, power plants, transmission lines, cooling systems,

semiconductor facilities and critical-mineral supply chains. No doubt, the AI revolution
has environmental costs. As adoption accelerates, growing demand for power, water,
advanced hardware and raw materials could strain grids, raise emissions and intensify
pressure on local resources. Yet the sustainability conversation should extend beyond
AI’s footprint to consider whether its environmental benefits can outweigh its
resource demands.
AI’s footprint is large, growing and certainly not trivial. AI increases electricity demand,

adds large, concentrated loads to already constrained grids, and may slow the retirement
of polluting energy assets. U.S. data centers could account for 20% of national electricity
consumption by 2035, up from 5.9% today, with power needs reaching 194 gigawatts
(comparable to the electricity demand of more than 150 million homes). 1 Cooling, power
generation and semiconductor manufacturing require water; each new megawatt also
embeds substantial copper, aluminum and other materials; and short hardware cycles

can increase electronic waste. Use varies sharply by workload: Complex reasoning and
agentic tasks can consume many times more energy than a simple text prompt. The
effects are already reaching consumers and communities. Wholesale power costs on
PJM—the largest U.S. grid—rose 76% year-over-year in Q1 2026, while data-center
load included in its previous two capacity auctions added an estimated $13.8 billion to
customer bills. 2 Communities are mobilizing around their concerns, including affordability,

water use and air pollution: Q1 2026 produced the largest single quarterly concentration
of delayed or blocked data-center projects on record. 3 Opposition is therefore becoming
a development constraint to be considered.
Context, caveats and comparisons. Headlines often tell only part of the story. Globally,
data centers account for about 1% of electricity use, 4 modest relative to many industries,
and 9% of estimated global 2025 to 2030 electricity growth. 5 A typical text prompt on

one major company’s AI model 6 uses 0.24 watt-hours of electricity, emits 0.03 grams
of CO₂ and consumes 0.26 milliliters of water—roughly 617,000 prompts match the
emissions from producing and cooking one sirloin steak, while 262,000 match the water
used in an eight-minute shower. 7 Still, billions of interactions, more complex workloads
and growth in resource-constrained regions can create meaningful cumulative impacts.
The most meaningful assessment therefore depends on workload, utilization, location,

grid mix, cooling design, local water availability and who bears the cost of supporting
infrastructure, not one universal number.
AI’s Handprint. While much remains to be seen, AI’s environmental “handprint”—the
benefits it can enable—could potentially prove larger than its direct footprint in some
areas. Across the energy system, AI can help utilities better match electricity supply
and demand, balance grids, optimize storage and reduce wasted energy. Data centers

already account for more than 30% of corporate renewable power-purchase agreements,
helping create long-dated demand for clean generation. 8 Beyond power, AI can improve
precision agriculture, streamline transportation and inventories, and support product

investment in power generation,

advanced cooling, water treatment
and reuse, efficient computing, and
materials recovery and recycling.
Diversified environmental and thematic
strategies may offer exposure to
these physical enablers while reducing
reliance on benchmark names.

design, predictive maintenance, waste sorting and recycling. AI is transforming climate
risk management by using satellite imagery, weather data and predictive analytics to
forecast floods, wildfires and extreme weather events, enabling governments, companies,
and investors to anticipate risks and allocate capital more effectively (Exhibit 2). These
gains are not automatic: they depend on innovation, adoption, and whether efficiency

improvements reduce total resource use rather than simply stimulate more demand. All
industries are faced with the opportunity to responsibly use AI to make operations more
efficient, and much is yet to be discovered.

AI’s potential handprint becomes more tangible in transportation, agriculture and climate
resilience. In aviation, for example, AI can combine weather forecasts, flight paths and
atmospheric conditions to reroute aircraft around regions where warming contrails
are most likely to form—reducing climate impact without requiring a new aircraft or

fuel system. On farms, machine learning can interpret aerial imagery and field-level
sensor data to identify crop stress early, directing water, fertilizer and chemicals to
where they are needed and helping growers improve yields while reducing inputs. AI
can also strengthen climate preparedness by analyzing satellite imagery, weather data
and infrastructure exposure to detect deforestation, anticipate floods or wildfires,
and identify communities, facilities and supply chains most vulnerable to physical

hazards. These are just a few examples that illustrate how AI’s value can extend beyond
incremental efficiency gains to reshape real-world decisions.
Investment Considerations. The challenges created by AI also create potential
investment opportunities. Companies are investing in efficient chips, closed-loop cooling,
reclaimed-water systems, energy storage, grid upgrades and cleaner firm power to
reduce resource intensity and improve reliability. Professionally managed environmental

and thematic strategies can target these “picks and shovels” of the digital buildout,
alongside businesses using AI to improve efficiency across energy management,
water infrastructure, environmental services, waste management and compute. A
disciplined thematic approach can diversify exposure beyond the largest benchmark
constituents while positioning for structural growth in power demand, grid modernization
and resource efficiency. Focusing solely on AI’s footprint risks overlooking both the

companies helping it scale more efficiently and the broader environmental benefits its
handprint may enable.

THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
The Beauty of September
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Call it an eventful first 13 trading days of September: Brent crude re-eclipsed $100 per
barrel, diesel prices surpassed $6 per gallon for the first time in history, the 10-year U.S.
Treasury yield breached 5%, 9 and the Fed joined the ECB and the BoJ in hiking rates for

the first time since July 2023. Through it all, the S&P 500 has shed 1.4% since the start
of the month, while its equal-weighted counterpart is down by 3.3%.
It’s not all too unexpected that September would be a choppy month for U.S. Equities.
Historical seasonal patterns suggest as much. Since 1928, the average S&P 500 return
in September has come out to -1.1%, 10 not only the worst month of the year on average
but also the only month when the benchmark has ended lower more often than it’s

ended higher. Similar weakness has been seen in midterm election years, with the S&P
500 producing an average September return of -0.8% since 1950. 10
The good news is that this has historically been an attractive entry point for U.S. Equity
investors. Call it the beauty of September: In contrast to September’s average decline,
average returns improve in October (+3.0%), November (+2.7%), and December (+0.8%).
Indeed, the S&P 500 has never been negative in the six or 12 months post-midterm

election since 1950 (Exhibit 4A), boasting an impressive average return of 13.8% and
15.8% over those respective periods. 10
Looking ahead to the coming weeks, we have little reason to believe that 2026
will diverge from the historical trend. Volatility is likely to rise into October before
moderating post-election, in line with what’s typical in the months surrounding the
midterms (Exhibit 4B). A quieter corporate calendar ahead of Q3 earnings means

investor focus will remain on incoming macro data, the path of energy prices, and the
Fed’s policy path.
In the meantime, while the S&P 500 still stands just 2.7% below its mid-August high,
churn beneath the surface already appears to be taking place. Just 38% of S&P 500
members now stand above their 50-day moving average versus 70% a mere month ago. 9
Far from worrisome, a correction in the near term in the context of a strong fundamental
economic backdrop would be a potential buying opportunity in our view.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

Document 208: Coreweave是否会引爆美国垃圾债市场？下一个是甲骨文？

Source PDF: all report pdf/Coreweave是否会引爆美国垃圾债市场？下一个是甲骨文？.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 7

### Page 1

CoreWeave 债券收益率飙⾄ 16% ，五年期 CDS 隐含违约概率达 63% ，与雷曼破产前⽔平相
当，直接威胁由其租⾦⽀撑的 141 亿数据中⼼垃圾债。德银认为，更危险的是甲⻣⽂ ——
BBB 评级悬于投资级末端， CDS 两⽇暴涨 36 基点，⼀旦降级将触发机构强制抛售，危机将
从垃圾债蔓延⾄投资级市场腹地。
摘要：
AI 基础设施融资链条的裂缝正在从边缘向中⼼蔓延。
CoreWeave 债券已深陷困境，其作为租⼾⽀撑着⾼收益市场逾 140 亿美元数据中⼼债券的偿付
能⼒，⽽德意志银⾏的全市场信⽤图谱显⽰， AI 信⽤分化正在加速 ——AA 级科技巨头依然享受
准主权级融资成本，但处于投资级末端的甲⻣⽂ CDS 已急剧⾛阔，⼀旦遭遇降级，将触发⼤规
模强制抛售，将这场 " 边缘危机 " 推⼊市场腹地。
CoreWeave 的债券已连续三⽇在困境价位交易。其 9.625% 、 2032 年到期票据收益率收盘约
14% ，据路透报道， 2031 年票据收益率更超过 16%—— 这⼀⽔平通常被视为困境信号。五年期

CDS 收于 1117 基点，隐含五年内违约概率约 63% ，与雷曼兄弟申请破产前数⽇的 CDS ⽔平相
当。2032 年票据⾃ 6 ⽉发⾏以来价格已跌破 80 美分，触及市场惯例定义的困境区间。
问题的核⼼不⽌于 CoreWeave ⾃⾝。CoreWeave 同时是⾼收益市场 20 只项⽬融资型数据中⼼债
券中七只的唯⼀租⼾，涉及债务规模 141 亿美元，占该市场总量的 31% 。这意味着，
CoreWeave 的资本成本危机，直接传导⾄ Applied Digital 、 Core Scientific 、 Galaxy Digital 等发
⾏⼈的债券偿付能⼒。
与此同时，德意志银⾏警告，甲⻣⽂五年期 CDS 两⽇内飙升 36 基点⾄ 288 基点，若其 BBB 评级遭
下调，将迫使只能持有投资级资产的机构投资者强制抛售，引发系统性连锁反应。
垃圾债市场的 AI 敞⼝： 141 亿美元悬在 CoreWeave ⼀⾝
据摩根⼠丹利最新⽉报《 AI 融资现状：暂停，⽽⾮撤退》，⾼收益市场中⾄少存在 20 只项⽬融资

型数据中⼼债券，合计债务规模 456 亿美元，为 4063 兆⽡关键 IT 容量提供融资，每只债券由单⼀
特殊⽬的实体发⾏，偿债来源为单⼀租⼾的租⾦⽀付。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

CoreWeave 是其中规模最⼤的租⼾，也是最脆弱的⼀环。 在上述 20 只债券中， CoreWeave 作
为租⼾的债券共七只，涉及债务 141 亿美元、 1512 兆⽡容量，占总量的 31% 和 37% ，超过⾕歌背
书的 Fluidstack 系列（ 119 亿美元）和英伟达（ 84 亿美元），成为⾼收益数据中⼼市场最⼤的单⼀
租⾦来源。
依赖 CoreWeave 租⾦的具体发⾏⼈包括：
Applied Digital （ APLD ）：两只债券合计 39.4 亿美元（ 23.5 亿美元 9.25% 2030 年票据及 15.9
亿美元 7% 2031 年票据，均位于北达科他州 Ellendale 的 Polaris Forge 项⽬），另有 21.5 亿美元
Polaris Forge 2 债券以甲⻣⽂为租⼾；
Core Scientific （ CORZ ）： 33 亿美元，为 590 兆⽡容量融资，分布于德克萨斯、佐治亚、俄
克拉荷⻢和北卡罗来纳，是追踪名单中 CoreWeave 租赁容量最⼤的单⼀债券；

Galaxy Digital （ GLXY ）： 35.1 亿美元 9.875% 票据，⽤于德克萨斯州拉伯克的 Helios 园区，
最终交付期⾄ 2028 年 3 ⽉；
Edged（ 13 亿美元， CoreWeave 与阿⾥巴巴共同租赁）、Elk Grove Village（ 9 亿美元）及
DDC 01 Propco（ 11.2 亿美元，弗吉尼亚州⾥⼠满），其中最后⼀只于 9 ⽉ 23 ⽇定价，即市场
剧烈动荡前三周。

### Page 3

这是双重敞⼝，⽽⾮简单的循环融资。以 Galaxy 债券持有⼈为例，其同时承担拉伯克的建设⻛
险，以及 CoreWeave ⾃ 2028 年起能否持续⽀付租⾦的信⽤⻛险。
市场已按租⼾定价：利差分化⼀⽬了然
摩根⼠丹利的利差数据清晰呈现了市场的判断。截⾄ 9 ⽉ 28 ⽇，七只 CoreWeave 租⼾债券平均
利差约 400 基点，是四只⾕歌背书 Fluidstack 交易（ Cipher 、 TeraWulf 、 Flash 、 Meridian ，约
195 基点）的两倍，也远⾼于亚⻢逊租赁债券（约 150 基点）。同⼀资产类别、同⼀结构、相近评
级，唯⼀的变量是租约上的名字，以及是否有超⼤规模云服务商联署担保。
利差最宽的债券有共同特征： Zenith Arc （ Jane Street 租⼾，俄克拉荷⻢， 2028 年 10 ⽉最终交
付）利差 599 基点、收益率 11.09% ； Galaxy 利差 559 基点 /10.68% ； Edged 利差 498 基点 /9.92%

（⾃发⾏以来扩⼤ 160 基点）； SoftBank 租赁的 SE Cosmos 利差 451 基点。弱租⼾、漫⻓建设周
期，或两者兼具，是共同的⻛险因⼦。
值得注意的是， CoreWeave 租⼾债券中表现最好的，是交付期最近的⼀只 ——Applied Digital 原
始 9.25% 2030 年票据，最终交付期为 2026 年 10 ⽉ 31 ⽇，报价 104.75 、利差 218 基点。距离通电
交付越近，市场对租⼾信⽤的敏感度越低；交付期越远，租⼾信⽤的权重越⾼。
此外，摩根⼠丹利的贷款成本⽐（ LTC ）与到期收益率散点图显⽰， 2026 年⼆、三季度发⾏的
债券（即热潮顶峰时期）集中在 LTC 85%-95% 、到期收益率 13%-17% 区间，即最新发⾏的债券
股权缓冲最薄、要求回报率最⾼，⻛险敞⼝最为集中。

### Page 4

德意志银⾏绘制全市场图谱： AI 信⽤已现三级分化
德意志银⾏研究院对美元投资级和⾼收益指数中每⼀只债券按收益率与久期作图，并标注 AI 相关
名称，得出了迄今最为系统的 AI 信⽤全景图。
结论是： AI 融资成本整体尚未失控，但曲线远端的分化正在扩⼤。
德银将 AI 信⽤划分为三个层级：
AA 级 AI 名称（ Meta 、 Alphabet 、英伟达、亚⻢逊）：短端融资成本约 5%-5.5% ，处于投资级
分布底部，市场仍将其视为准主权级借款⼈；
BBB 级 AI 名称（甲⻣⽂、 SpaceX ）： 10-12 年久期融资成本约 8%-8.5% ，处于投资级分布顶
端，较同久期债券⾼出约 1.5-2 个百分点，形成明显的 "AI 税 " ；
⾼收益 AI 名称：多为 BB 级，收益率 7%-11% ，⼤体处于⾼收益市场中游。
CoreWeave 是唯⼀的异类。在整个⾼收益指数中，收益率最⾼的债券（ 15%-19% ）通常与 AI ⽆
关，但 CoreWeave 是例外 —— 其 BB+ 评级的⽆担保 2032 年票据，⾃发⾏仅四个⽉便跌破 80 美

分，五年期 CDS 收于 1118 基点。

### Page 5

德银指出， CoreWeave 以四⾄六年 " 照付不议 " 合同出租算⼒，待履⾏订单超过 1000 亿美元，其
中约三分之⼆来⾃微软、 Meta 、 IBM 、卡特彼勒等投资级客⼾。但债券市场显然更在意杠杆
率，⽽⾮客⼾名单。
德银还指出⼀个被市场忽视的结构性⽭盾：先进 GPU 芯⽚的折旧周期约为四⾄六年，与合同期
限吻合，但为其融资的债务期限更⻓。
这意味着抵押品在债务到期前已经贬值，这正是收益率曲线远端利差扩⼤的根本原因。叠加市
场重新定价美联储加息预期，期限溢价将成为所有需要再融资的借款⼈的额外负担。
甲⻣⽂：下⼀个多⽶诺⻣牌？
德意志银⾏明确将甲⻣⽂列为下⼀个需要密切关注的⻛险节点。
该⾏警告， " 这种担忧可能成为⾃我实现的预⾔，并有引发其他 AI 相关名称更⼴泛问题的⻛险 " ，
并将甲⻣⽂列为 " ⻛向标 " ：其五年期 CDS 在两⽇内飙升 36 基点⾄ 288 基点，⾼盛数据显⽰周三单
⽇扩⼤ 31 基点。
甲⻣⽂的系统性⻛险在于其评级位置。甲⻣⽂⽬前处于 BBB 评级，即投资级最低档。⼀旦遭遇

降级，⼤量只能持有投资级资产的机构投资者将被迫强制抛售，这不是有序的重新定价，⽽是
⼀次强制性的流动性冲击。
从债券市场来看，两只甲⻣⽂租⼾债券（ Applied Digital 的 Polaris Forge 2 和 Yondr 的 Loudoun
County 项⽬）的利差，已较同期限甲⻣⽂⾃⾝⽆担保债券宽出 185-220 基点， Yondr 利差⼀度在
9 ⽉初扩⼤⾄近 300 基点。摩根⼠丹利上周警告，数据中⼼交付延迟可能严重冲击甲⻣⽂的债务
状况。
融资缺⼝与多空分歧
CoreWeave 的核⼼⽭盾在于⼀个 350 亿美元的融资缺⼝。
德意志银⾏分析师 Brad Zelnick 在题为 " 错位带来买⼊机会 " 的报告中重申对 CoreWeave 的买⼊评
级和 150 美元⽬标价，指出公司⼆季度末可⽤流动性 155 亿美元，待履⾏订单中 60%-65% 来⾃投
资级客⼾，新合同的⽆杠杆内部收益率已从约 15% 跳升⾄ " 约 20% 以上 " 。但他同时承认关键⽭
盾：
CoreWeave 未来 12-18 个⽉内⽆疑需要额外资本以履⾏现有订单，未来 12 个⽉经营现⾦流约 100

亿美元，⽽所需资本⽀出约 450 亿美元，缺⼝约 350 亿美元。

### Page 6

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

写评论
若这⼀缺⼝的哪怕⼀半需要以当前约 16% 的⽆担保债券利率融资，额外利息⽀出将约达 28 亿美
元 / 年，超过分析师预期 CoreWeave 今年全年 28 亿美元亏损的总额。
⾼盛的 Rich Privorotsky 倾向于认为市场 “ 在局部过度反应 ” ，认为 “ 存在合理的融资约束，但这并
不否定底层需求逻辑 ” 。摩根⼠丹利则认为 “ 对于⾼质量⾼收益债券，当利率企稳时⻛险回报看起
来不错 ” 。
⾼盛和摩根⼠丹利的关键前提词均是 " ⾼质量 " 和 " 如果利率企稳 "—— ⽽在 10 年期美债收益率仍
⾼于 5% 、加息预期升温的背景下，这两个条件对于拉伯克⼀座唯⼀租⼾ CDS 已定价为违约的
BB- 级建筑外壳⽽⾔，均不成⽴。
传导路径：从边缘到中⼼

综合摩根⼠丹利和德银的两份报告，当前 AI 信⽤⻛险的传导路径已相对清晰：
第⼀层是 CoreWeave ⾃⾝ —— ⽆担保债券收益率 16% ， CDS 隐含违约概率超六成，融资缺⼝
350 亿美元；
第⼆层是依赖 CoreWeave 租⾦的 141 亿美元⾼收益数据中⼼债券 ——Galaxy 、 Core Scientific 、
Applied Digital 、 Edged 等发⾏⼈，其中建设周期越⻓、交付期越远的债券⻛险越⾼；
第三层是甲⻣⽂ ——BBB 评级悬于投资级末端， CDS 持续⾛阔，⼀旦降级将触发强制抛售，将
危机从⾼收益市场蔓延⾄投资级市场腹地；
第四层是购买这些债券的寿险公司等机构投资者 —— 它们是这类项⽬融资债券的最⼤买家，正
在重新审视超⼤规模云服务商名称出现在募集说明书中究竟意味着什么样的信⽤背书。
德意志银⾏的结论与⾼盛 Privorotsky 的判断⾼度⼀致：
信⽤危机总是从边缘开始。 2007 年，次贷也曾被认为是 “ 可控的 ” 。
“ 随着资本⽀出周期从⾃由现⾦流转向债务融资，我们必须越来越尊重信⽤市场的价格信号。 AI

融资成本整体上并不极端，但边缘已出现令⼈担忧的主题。 ”

Document 254: IMF警告日本可能需要更快加息，全球债市再添压力？

Source PDF: all report pdf/IMF警告日本可能需要更快加息，全球债市再添压力？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-17
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

IMF 罕⻅背书⽇本货币政策正常化，并提⽰若通胀超预期央⾏需加快加息。⽬前⽇本政策利
率已升⾄ 1.25% ，市场预计年内或再加息。随着⽇债收益率上升，⽇本资⾦加速撤离法债
等海外资产，加剧了全球债市的波动与承压。
摘要：
国际货币基⾦组织罕⻅地为⽇本货币政策正常化背书，并警告若通胀超预期，⽇本央⾏可能需
要加快加息步伐。这⼀表态，在全球债市已然动荡之际，令市场对⽇本资⾦撤离海外资产的担
忧进⼀步升温。
IMF 亚太部⻔主任 Krishna Srinivasan 在本周于曼⾕召开的 IMF 年会期间接受路透社采访时表⽰，
⽇本央⾏ " 正在做正确的事 " ，并强调若通胀上升快于预期， " 必须更快收紧，因为你不希望通胀
预期脱锚。这取决于数据。 "⽇本央⾏⾏⻓植⽥和男亦在出席本次 IMF 年会时重申，央⾏将专注
于将核⼼通胀锚定在 2% ⽬标附近，不会容忍任何超调，进⼀步强化了市场对年内再度加息的预
期。
IMF 的表态恰逢全球债市承压之际。法国 10 年期国债收益率⼀度逼近 5% ，创 2002 年以来新⾼，

借贷成本已⾼于希腊和意⼤利；美国 10 年期国债收益率攀升⾄ 5.28% 的数⼗年⾼位，即便通胀
数据⾛软也未能遏制抛售势头。在部分市场分析⼈⼠看来，⽇本政策正常化及由此引发的⽇本
资⾦回流，或是这两场动荡背后不可忽视的结构性⼒量。
⽇本央⾏正常化提速预期升温
⽇本央⾏已于今年 6 ⽉和 9 ⽉相继加息，将政策利率推升⾄ 1.25% ，为 31 年来最⾼。多数分析师
预计年内还将有⾄少⼀次加息。
Srinivasan 指出，⽇元疲软进⼀步推⾼了原材料进⼝成本，加剧了国内通胀压⼒，这也正是促
使⽇本央⾏持续收紧政策的重要背景之⼀。⽇本央⾏本⽉晚些时候将召开关键政策会议，市场
普遍预计届时利率将维持不变，但可能释放鹰派信号，为 12 ⽉加息铺路。
此外，中东冲突带来的通胀⻛险和⼈⼯智能需求旺盛，也在本次 IMF 年会上成为各国政策制定者
讨论的重要议题。
IMF ：⽇元⼲预不违背浮动汇率承诺
在汇率问题上， IMF 对⽇本当局的⽴场给予背书。 Srinivasan 表⽰，⽇本当局 " 完全致⼒于⾃由浮

动汇率制度 " ，他与⽇⽅进⾏了多次讨论，对此 " 毫不怀疑 " 。
IMF 在其亚洲经济报告的分析章节中指出， 2025 年年中以来⽇元的⼤幅波动，很⼤程度上与收
益率差等基本⾯因素关联不⼤，⽽是与⽇元期货的投机性仓位同步移动。⽇本于今年 7 ⽉ 31 ⽇与
美国联合进⾏了⼀次罕⻅的协调⼲预，以阻⽌⽇元跌⾄近 40 年低点。 Srinivasan 表⽰，⽇本汇市
深度充⾜，当局将汇率波动作为 " 第⼀道防线 " ，⼲预仅在特定情况下使⽤。
除货币政策外，⽇本的财政可持续性同样令市场存有疑虑。市场担⼼⽇本政府的⼤规模⽀出计
划，叠加明年 4 ⽉起为期两年的消费税下调，将进⼀步扩⼤本已庞⼤的债务规模。
Srinivasan 承认市场对财政⻛险的担忧，但也指出，疫情以来⽇本的财政整合⼒度实际上已超过
其他任何⼀个 G7 国家，部分原因在于税收收⼊的持续增⻓。他同时强调，⽇本需要明确新⽀出
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

⽇本资⾦撤离或加剧全球债市波动
全球债市的动荡，与⽇本政策正常化进程之间的关联正受到越来越多的关注。据彭博专栏作家
Gearoid Reidy 在分析⽂章中指出，世界或许并未为 " 有利率的⽇本 " 做好充分准备，⽽法国可能
只是第⼀个受到冲击的市场。
数据显⽰，截⾄今年 7 ⽉，⽇本投资者持有约 23 万亿⽇元（约合 1450 亿美元）法国国债，是⽇本
在欧元区最⼤的超配仓位。然⽽随着⽇本 10 年期国债收益率上⽉升破 3% ，创 30 年新⾼，经汇率
对冲后，法国 10 年期国债相对⽇本国债的收益率优势已收窄⾄约 40 个基点，吸引⼒⼤幅下滑。
收益率优势消退叠加法国财政状况恶化，⽇本资⾦已开始松动。数据显⽰，⽇本持有的法国国

债规模较去年底减少 2.5% 。三井住友 DS 资产管理旗下由 Shinji Kunibe 负责的全球固收基⾦已出
于财政担忧清仓法债。瑞穗的 Masayuki Nakajima 表⽰： " 即便估值看起来更便宜，⽇本投资者
重建仓位的动⼒也在下降。 "
随着 IMF 为⽇本加快加息提供政策⽀撑，⽇本国内资产吸引⼒的持续回升，或将使这⼀趋势延
续，并对依赖⽇本资⾦的海外债券市场形成持续的结构性压⼒。

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
 Markets regained some footing to close off the week after 
President Trump said the US would not strike Iran before 
next month’s midterm elections, supporting risk sentiment 
and helping equities recover. However, tensions flared again 
over the weekend, with an attack on Saudi Arabia’s main 
airport and a US strike on a commercial tanker in the Gulf of 
Oman. On Friday, President Trump also announced that Russia 
would supply 13.5 million barrels of diesel to global markets 
through October to help ease fuel shortages and lower prices, 
a move that would mark a significant departure from years 
of sanctions despite the ongoing war in Ukraine. US bond 
markets will be closed on Monday for Columbus Day, although 
equity markets remain open. 
• US equities closed higher on Friday, capping a volatile week 
that saw sharp moves in both bond yields and energy markets. 
The S&P 500 rose 0.6%, although it remained below the 
record high reached earlier in the week. The Dow Jones gained 
0.8%, while the Nasdaq also advanced 0.8%, rebounding 
from Thursday’s tech-led sell-off amid concerns over the pace 
of AI-related growth. European equities outperformed as 
volatility in regional bond markets eased, with the Euro Stoxx 
50 rising 0.8% and the FTSE 100 up 1.1%.
• Asian equities delivered a mixed performance on Friday. The 
Nikkei 225 closed flat, recovering from an intraday decline of 
1.3% after reports that OpenAI’s revenue targets may indeed 
be reached. The CSI 300 was up 0.2%, while the Hang Seng 
rose 1.8%, supported by gains in technology stocks. The ASX 
200 advanced 0.6%, with futures pointing to a further gain at 
today’s open. South Korea’s Kospi was closed for Hangul Day.
• US Treasury yields were little changed as investors digested 
President Trump’s pledge not to take military action against 
Iran until after the midterm elections. The 2-year and 10-
year Treasury rose 3bps and 1bp respectively, with the latter 
holding at 5.24%, below the 24-year high reached earlier in 
the week. Attention now turns to the September CPI report, 
due Wednesday night (11:30pm AEDT), and comments from 
Fed Chair Warsh, both of which are expected to shape market 
expectations ahead of the FOMC’s October meeting. Futures 
currently assign a 17% probability to an October rate hike and 
have almost fully priced in a hike by December, leaving the 
implied year-end policy rate at 4.13%. 
•  European bonds ended lower after a volatile week. The 
10-year bund yield fell 1bp, while the 10-year gilts declined 
4bps. France’s 10-year OAT yield also dropped 4bps, although 
concerns over the government’s ability to reduce the budget 
deficit to 5% of GDP by 2027 continue to weigh on sentiment.
• In Australia, the 3-year government bond yield was 
unchanged at 4.90%, while the 10-year yield fell 6bps to 
5.34%. Attention this week will centre on the September 
Labour Force Survey, due Thursday, where a moderation in 
the participation rate from its near 115-year high is expected 
to keep the unemployment rate broadly steady (see our 
preview). Swap markets continue to assign only a modest 
probability to further policy tightening, with around 26% 
odds of a November rate hike currently priced.
• The US dollar index rose modestly, gaining 0.1%. The AUD 
rose against the greenback, up 0.3% to 0.6978. Other crosses 
against the USD were mixed, with the yen up 0.2%, euro 
losing 0.2% and the pound flat.
• Commodities were mostly higher. Oil prices recovered from 
early losses, with Brent crude rising 0.4% to US$104.7/bbl 
and WTI also gaining 0.4%. Elsewhere, gold advanced 1.5% 
and copper rose 1.6%, while iron ore bucked the trend, falling 
0.7% amid a subdued outlook for demand. 
International Data
In the US, the University of Michigan consumer sentiment 
deteriorated marginally in the preliminary October print, the 
headline index declining from 48.1 to 46.3. Current conditions 
fell from 50.9 to 44.7 while expectations edged up to 47.3. 
Historically low sentiment is yet to stop consumers from 
spending though, with real consumption growth remaining 
robust through 2026.

Today’s key data and events

- CN M2 Money Supply Sep 7.4% 7.5%

中華民國一一五年九月七日

國際經濟金融週報

### Page 2

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

1

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國就業回穩支撐景氣韌性，惟通膨壓力與貿易逆差擴大增添政策不確定性 
經濟 
⚫ 8 月美國非農就業增加 16.2 萬人，優於前月及近一年平均，失業率維持
4.1%，就業市場呈現溫和增長，整體表現仍具韌性。新增就業以餐飲服
務、地方政府教育及製造業為主，惟資訊業仍減少人力。 
⚫ 7 月美國職位空缺數增至 727.1 萬個 ， 職缺率由4.3%升至 4.4%； 惟聘僱
及總離職人數分別減少 27.8 萬人及 26.5 萬人，其中主動離職人數減少
15.7 萬人，裁員與解僱人數減少11.9 萬人。整體來看，企業用人需求尚
屬穩健，但招募及勞工流動動能略有降溫，勞動市場供需仍維持平衡。 
⚫ 8 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數為 54.6，雖較上月下降 1.0 點，但
已連續八個月維持擴張。新訂單、就業、未完成訂單及進口指數回落，
顯示需求動能有所放緩 ； 惟生產與出口持續擴張 ， 供應商交貨時間延長 ，
原物料價格仍處高檔，整體製造業活動仍維持擴張態勢。 
⚫ 8 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數由 54.1 升至 55.4，連續第 26 個月
維持擴張。商業活動與新訂單指數分別升至 61.7 及 60.9，反映服務業
需求仍具支撐；惟雇傭指數連續兩個月低於 50，企業聘僱態度仍較審
慎。此外，價格指數升至 72.6，創 2022 年 8 月以來新高，通膨與成本
壓力依然偏高。 
⚫ 7 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 886 億美元，較上月增加 24.4%，
主因出口減少 2.1%、進口增加 2.8%。出口下滑主要受原油及非貨幣黃
金等工業原料出口減少影響；進口則受電腦、半導體等資本財需求增加
帶動。累計前 7 月貿易逆差較去年同期縮減 29.6%，顯示美國對外貿易
失衡情況明顯改善。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 美國於 8 月 30 日以伊朗革命衛隊疑似準備在荷姆茲海峽部署水雷、威
脅商業航運安全為由，對伊朗拉拉克島軍事設施發動精準打擊。伊朗隨
後向約旦境內美軍基地發射飛彈及無人機報復，區域緊張情勢明顯升
溫。由於荷姆茲海峽為全球重要原油運輸樞紐，市場對能源供應與航運
安全的疑慮加深，帶動國際油價及避險需求走高，並增添全球通膨及金
融市場不確定性。 
短評 ⚫ 8 月非農就業優於預期 ， 失業率維持低檔 ， 勞動市場尚未明顯轉弱 ；7 月

一、 美國—就業回穩支撐景氣韌性，惟通膨壓力與貿易逆差擴大增添政策不確
定性 
2026 年 8 月美國就業市場呈現回穩跡象，非農就業人數較上月增加 16.2 萬
人，不僅高於上月修正後的 2.1 萬人，也明顯優於過去 12 個月平均每月增加 3.1
萬人的水準；失業率則維持於 4.1%，失業人數約 703.1 萬人，顯示勞動市場整體
尚未明顯轉弱。從產業表現來看，新增就業主要集中於餐飲服務業增加 5.9 萬人
及地方政府教育部門增加 4.2 萬人 ， 製造業亦增加1.6 萬人 ， 持續支撐就業成長 ；
醫療保健業增加 1.3 萬人，雖維持擴張，但增幅低於過去一年平均水準。另一方
面 ， 資訊業減少2.3 萬人 ， 為主要拖累來源 ， 反映部分產業仍處於人力調整階段 。
勞動市場結構方面，勞動參與率回升至 61.6%，較前月改善，但仍較今年 1 月低
0.5 個百分點；就業人口比率則上升至 59.1%。薪資與工時方面，民營部門平均每
週工時升至 34.4 小時，平均時薪增至 37.75 美元，月增 0.3%、年增 3.1%，薪資
成長保持溫和。整體而言，8 月就業數據顯示美國勞動市場在前月疲弱後回穩，
且前兩個月數據上修，反映就業市場仍具韌性。強勁就業報告公布後，美債殖利
率走升，市場對聯準會 9 月維持偏緊政策的預期升溫，升息可能性亦有所提高。 
2026 年 7 月美國職位空缺數為 727.1 萬個，較上月增加 8.9 萬個，職缺率由
4.3%升至 4.4%；聘僱人數為 505.4 萬人，較上月減少 27.8 萬人，聘僱率由 3.4%
降至 3.2%；總離職人數為 507.2 萬人，較上月減少 26.5 萬人，離職率亦由 3.4%
降至 3.2%，顯示勞動市場流動性有所降溫。就離職結構觀察，主動離職人數為
305.6 萬人，較上月減少 15.7 萬人；裁員與解僱人數為 166.6 萬人，較上月減少
11.9 萬人 。 產業別方面 ， 職位空缺增加主要集中於耐久財製造業(增加 7.6 萬個)；
聘僱人數則以專業及商業服務業減少 18.8 萬人最為明顯 ； 主動離職人數以住宿及
餐飲服務業減少 8.6 萬人最多。整體而言，7 月美國職缺小幅增加，顯示企業用
人需求仍具支撐，惟聘僱及離職規模同步縮減，反映勞動市場流動性趨緩；加以
主動離職率及裁員率雙雙下降，顯示勞工轉職意願與企業人力調整均趨於審慎，
勞動市場供需大致維持平衡。 
2026 年 8 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI)為 54.6，較

上月的 55.6 下降 1.0 點，惟已連續第八個月維持在 50 以上擴張區間，顯示美國
製造業景氣持續擴張。細項觀察，新訂單指數由56.7 降至 53.7，雖連續第八個月
處於擴張區間，但需求動能較前月放緩；生產指數由58.5 微降至 58.3，連續第十
個月擴張，產出活動維持穩健；就業指數由52.8 降至 51.2，仍處擴張區間，反映
企業聘僱需求持續增加。供應商交貨指數由58.9 升至 59.3，供應商交貨時間持續
延長；庫存指數則由 51.2 降至 50.6，企業仍持續累積庫存，但備貨動能較前月趨
緩。此外，價格指數維持71.1 不變，原物料價格仍處相對高檔；新出口訂單指數
由 53.0 微升至 53.2， 連續第二個月維持擴張 ； 惟未完成訂單指數由55.0 降至 51.8，
進口指數亦由 55.7 降至 52.5，顯示部分需求與投入動能有所降溫。整體而言，8
月美國製造業活動雖仍維持擴張，但擴張力道較前月略有減弱，新訂單、就業、
未完成訂單及進口等指標同步回落，反映需求成長動能趨緩；另一方面，供應商
交貨時間持續延長、原物料價格維持高檔，加以中東地緣政治衝突、關稅政策及
供應鏈不確定性仍存，可能持續對製造業活動形成壓力。 
2026 年 8 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI) 升至 55.4，較上
月 54.1 上升 1.3 個百分點，連續第26 個月維持在 50 以上擴張區間，且高於近12
個月平均值 53.7，顯示美國服務業景氣持續穩健擴張。商業活動指數由59.1 升至
61.7，創 2022 年 11 月以來最高水準；新訂單指數由 57.2 升至 60.9，連續 15 個
月維持擴張，創 2023 年 2 月以來新高，顯示需求動能進一步增強。勞動市場方
面，雇傭指數由 47.4 微升至 47.8，但已連續兩個月處於收縮區間，反映企業聘僱
態度仍偏審慎。價格指數由 70.3 升至 72.6，創 2022 年 8 月以來最高水準，顯示
通膨與成本壓力依舊偏高。供應鏈方面，供應商交期指數由52.8 降至 51.3，雖連
續 21 個月高於 50，但已連續第四個月改善；庫存指數則由 51.4 升至 56.7，顯示
企業備貨意願增強。此外，新出口訂單指數及進口指數均升至 56.3，反映外部需
求與採購活動同步回溫。整體而言，8 月美國服務業景氣擴張力道進一步增強，
商業活動與新訂單表現亮眼，惟就業指數仍低於榮枯線，加上企業持續關注關稅
政策及中東衝突對供應鏈與成本的影響，後續仍須觀察通膨與勞動市場發展。 
2026 年 7 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 886 億美元 ， 較上月增加174 億
美元，增幅 24.4%。當月出口 3,107 億美元，較上月減少 66 億美元(-2.1%)；進口
3,993 億美元，較上月增加 108 億美元(+2.8%)。其中，商品貿易逆差擴大至1,196
億美元，較上月增加 17.3%；服務貿易順差則增至 310 億美元，增幅 0.7%。出口

下滑主要受工業用品及原材料出口減少 87 億美元影響，其中原油及非貨幣黃金

進口則增加 1.9%，顯示美國對外貿易失衡情況較去年同期明顯改善。 
8 月 30 日美軍以伊朗革命衛隊(IRGC)疑似準備於荷姆茲海峽部署水雷 、 威脅
商業航運安全為由，對伊朗拉拉克島(Larak Island) 火箭發射設施發動精準打擊，
為美方逾一個月來首度對伊朗本土目標採取軍事行動。伊朗隨後以飛彈及無人機
攻擊約旦與阿聯酋境內美軍相關設施作為回應，顯示衝突已由雙邊軍事對抗逐步
向區域層面擴散。至 9 月 5 日美國中央司令部(CENTCOM)表示，因伊朗革命衛
隊向兩艘美國海軍艦艇發射彈道飛彈，美軍已對 3 艘伊朗油輪發動攻擊，其中一
艘位於伊朗重要石油出口樞紐哈爾克島(Kharg Island)外海。美方稱未造成人員傷
亡，惟伊朗半官方媒體指出，哈爾克島錨地附近油輪遭 4 枚美軍飛彈擊中，船員
已展開撤離。由於哈爾克島為伊朗最重要的原油出口據點，戰前約九成原油出口
均經由該地，加上荷姆茲海峽為全球關鍵能源運輸通道，本次衝突已明顯升高市
場對油輪航行安全、原油供應穩定性及運輸成本上揚的憂慮，帶動布蘭特原油期
貨於 9 月 4 日收報每桶 96.28 美元，創 7 月 24 日以來新高。整體而言，美伊衝突
已由原先針對軍事設施的交鋒，進一步延伸至能源運輸與油輪目標，若後續報復
與反制行動持續升級，恐加劇中東原油供應中斷風險，推升全球能源價格與通膨
壓力，並提高市場對美國經濟前景及聯準會貨幣政策走向的不確定性。 
二、歐元區—通膨壓力回升，核心通膨走向值得關注 
 歐元區 7 月失業率持平 6.4%，失業人數持平上月水準。其中德國失業人數增
加 1.1 萬人，失業率持平為 4%；法國、義大利失業人數皆下降 1 萬人，失業率分
別持平為 8.3%、5.8%； 西班牙失業人數持平上月 ， 惟因部分勞動力退出勞動市場 ，
使失業率略降為 10.1%。整體而言，失業率維持在 6.3%-6.4%的低點區間波動，
且 8 月 PMI 的就業指數也有所增溫， 顯示勞動市場的韌性可望延續， 並支持消費
需求表現。 
 歐元區 8 月 HICP 通膨上升 0.4 個百分點至 3.3%，其中因燃料價格上揚，能
源通膨上升 4 個百分點至 14.3%，食品通膨則穩定於 1.2%，迄今能源及化肥價格

上漲仍未對食品價格造成明顯影響 ， 惟不排除存在滯後效應 。 核心通膨(不含食品

輕服務通膨上漲壓力 。 不過 ，8 月以來布蘭特原油大致位於每桶 85-95 美元區間 ，
預期年底前仍將對通膨帶來額外壓力，後續須關注能源漲價可能會透過運輸、物
流等成本管道傳導至核心商品及服務價格，進而提高核心通膨黏性。 
三、日本—製造業產出穩健成長，消費動能仍具韌性 
7 月零售月增 2.4%，部份反映 6 月受梅雨及豪雨天氣影響後的回升，顯示消
費動能仍具韌性。其中耐久財消費持續受到政策及汰舊換新需求支撐，家電銷售
在 6 月大幅下滑後明顯回升 ， 主要受空調等產品需求帶動 ； 汽車銷售額大致持平，
顯示政府自 4 月推動的稅務優惠措施效果尚未消退。另，非耐久財消費亦呈現全
面改善，服裝銷售受炎熱天氣帶動而大幅回升，一般商品銷售則反映國內消費及
觀光需求同步增強，食品銷售亦維持成長， 惟部分增幅可能並非實際需求增加，
若未來食品通膨進一步升溫，恐壓縮家庭實質購買力及消費信心，進而對後續非
耐久財支出及消費動能形成壓力。 
7 月工業生產月增 0.1%，主要係人工智慧相關需求帶動，科技與非科技產業
產出同步增加，其中半導體製造設備、電子零組件及化學工業表現尤為亮眼。出
貨月增 2.2%，高於生產增幅，而庫存僅增 0.5%、庫存率下降 1.7%，顯示產品去
化情況良好，整體供需維持穩健。展望未來，企業預估 8 月工業生產將大幅成長
6.4%，雖 9 月可能略為回落，但PMI 等前瞻指標仍顯示製造業活動持續擴張，預
計第三季製造業產出可望維持穩健成長。 
四、中國—8 月景氣略為回穩，但前景仍有較多不利因素 
8 月官方製造業 PMI 回升 0.6 點至 49.8，連續兩個月處於微幅衰退區間，新
訂單、出口訂單指數雖分別回升至 50.6、50.1，並帶動生產指數回到 50.4，不過，
庫存指數下降 、 就業指數加速衰退 、 企業展望減弱 ， 皆拖累PMI 指數水準 。 此外 ，
供應鏈交期指數上升至 50.1、投入物價指數回升至 56.6，反映交期拉長及成本加
速上漲，使業者營運承壓；產出物價指數回升 2.6 點至 50.4，售價調整速度依然

Document 346: When it comes to China, Europe should focus less on its trade deficits and more on its investments (2026-10-05)

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.36 / 5 October 2026EDITORIAL
WHEN IT COMES TO CHINA, EUROPE SHOULD FOCUS LESS ON ITS TRADE DEFICITS AND MORE ON ITS INVESTMENTS
1 See our analysis: Up against China’s industrial surge, Europe has strengths but is seeking a strategy.
On 8 and 9 October, the European Commissioner for Trade and Security, Maroš Šefcovic, will visit Beijing. The European Union’s
trade deficit with China on manufactured goods stands at EUR 400 billion over a twelve-month period, driven by a surge in

imports. That said, as a proportion of GDP , it is below its 2022 peak (2.1% compared with 2.5%). Furthermore, the aggregate
figure masks contrasting realities: 1) with the exception of the automotive sector and Germany, exports are holding up, in-
cluding service exports; 2) the loss of the Chinese market has been largely offset by third markets; and 3) the real challenge
lies in the move upmarket of products imported from China. More than simply rebalancing the bilateral trade balance, the

challenge for Europe is to defend its market more firmly and invest more at home to strengthen its competitiveness.
EXPORTS TO CHINA: A TARGETED, NOT WIDESPREAD, DECLINE
The decline in exports to China is not across the board. Since 2022, the
year when European exports to China peaked, the automotive sector
(vehicles and equipment) has accounted for two-thirds of the fall in
European sales in China (EUR 23bn out of 34bn), due to the surge in
electric vehicles and local brands on the Chinese market. Competition

has also intensified in the electronic-component sector, with a 40% fall
in European exports since 2022 — a sign of Beijing’s progress in its
substitution strategy and in bolstering its autonomy in key sectors such
as AI. In total, these two sectors (automotive and electronics) account
for more than 80% of the decline in European exports to China since
2022 (EUR 28bn out of 34bn).
The rest of the exports have held up significantly better, as they have

fallen by only 3% since 2022 (EUR -6bn) and have remained stable
over the past year. A number of sectors have even seen an increase,
including specialised machinery, aircraft engines and equipment, and
pharmaceuticals. The same has happened with high value-added ser -
vices, where the EU has seen its surplus with China increase in recent
years, despite stalling in 20251.
Germany — by far Europe’s leading exporter of cars to China — is
bearing the brunt of the impact. Its exports to China have fallen by

EUR 32bn (-30%) since 2022, accounting for the bulk of the European
decline (EUR 34bn). As a result, excluding Germany, exports by value
have remained stable since 2022 and have even started to rise again
this year (+5% y/y in the first half, compared with -12% for Germany;
see Chart 1). France and Italy recorded growth of over 15% in the first
half of the year, driven by chemicals (France and Italy) and aeronautics
(France). The decline seen in Germany is also having a heavy impact

through its value chains, as it is leading to a fall in exports from Central
European countries (including Slovakia, Slovenia and Bulgaria).
A SHIFT WELL UNDER WAY BUT FRAGILE
The contraction in the Chinese market has been largely offset
elsewhere. Excluding China, the EU’s manufacturing surplus is still
EUR 60bn higher than its 2022 level (see Chart 2), although it declined
in 2026 due to the fall in exports to the United States. European com -
panies have increased their exports to other destinations, particularly

within the continent. Exports of chemicals and pharmaceutical pro -
ducts to Switzerland, for example, have risen by a third since 2022.

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.36 / 5 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
As a result, the share of the EFTA 2 in extra-EU manufacturing exports
reached 11% in July 3, an all-time high, while China’s share fell below
8%, its lowest level since 2010. The United States’ share, by contrast,
fell back to 21%, 3 pp below its 2025 peak.
This reorientation is all the more valuable given that exports are also
losing momentum elsewhere, and not just in the United States. The

deterioration in Asian markets is not limited to China. Indeed, the ma-
nufacturing trade deficit with the ASEAN and Taiwan has also widened
significantly since 2022, with some countries acting in part as re-ex -
port hubs for Chinese goods. In particular, the EU’s bilateral deficit with
Vietnam has risen from EUR 36bn in 2022 to 57bn today, while the
deficit with Taiwan — a major exporter of electronic equipment, par -
ticularly AI-related products — has increased from EUR 15bn to 26bn.

That said, recent indicators are encouraging. The volume of EU goods
exports surprised on the upside in Q2 2026 (+4.3% q/q), driven —
though not exclusively — by Ireland (+2.1% q/q excluding Ireland). Sep-
tember business climate surveys also point to a marked improvement
in industrial export order books in the euro area, reaching their highest
levels since February 2022 according to the PMI and since October
2023 according to the European Commission survey.

EU IMPORTS FROM CHINA ARE THE CRUX OF THE MATTER
China is becoming an increasingly indispensable supplier for Europe
and therein lies the Achilles' heel of the EU-27. Imports from China
rose by a further 7% y/y in the first half of 2026, bringing the country’s
share of imports from outside the EU to 23% – a new record (31% for
manufactured goods alone).
These imports also reflect China’s move up the value chain — parti -
cularly in the automotive, electrical equipment, machinery, and phar -

maceutical sectors, where imports have almost doubled in value since
2022. Although the amounts remain modest at this stage (EUR 11bn),
their rapid growth is a ground for vigilance. The automotive sector has
demonstrated how quickly Chinese exports can break into the Euro -
pean market, given the significant competitiveness gaps — both in
terms of cost and, increasingly, non-cost factors. Furthermore, the EU
relies on China for certain strategic products (notably critical metals);

China could impose retaliatory measures regarding these goods in res-
ponse to potential European trade barriers, as illustrated by the 2025
restrictions on rare earth elements.
REBALANCING WILL NOT COME FROM TRADE ALONE
Europe’s response to China’s rising competitiveness cannot be limited
to the issue of the bilateral trade balance; doing so would risk repea -
ting the errors of the US administration, which made bilateral deficits
the be-all and end-all of its tariff policy. The imbalance is primarily on

Chinese’s side: its structurally high trade surplus (nearly 6% of GDP)
is driven by sluggish domestic demand, making exports an essential
outlet for its overproduction. China’s industrial strategy targets sectors
where Europe holds a dominant position, and there is no guarantee
that the European sectors currently holding their ground will continue
to do so in the future. Europe is right to take a tougher stance in order
to rebalance competition between the two blocs. In recent months, the

Commission has stepped up safeguard measures — covering chemi -
cals, automobiles, and steel, including the introduction of quotas and
price floors for electrical steel in September — while Paris and Berlin
are preparing a joint position to strengthen trade defence instruments,
to be unveiled at the October European Council. However, this firm ap-
proach has its limits, as imports of Chinese intermediate goods remain
essential to the competitiveness of European industry.

2 The European Free Trade Association, comprising Iceland, Liechtenstein, Norway and Switzerland.
3 Twelve-month rolling total.
4 According to the OECD, R&D expenditure accounted for 2.1% of GDP in Europe and 2.7% in China in 2024.
For Europe, another issue is its investment gap. The Draghi report
estimated the additional annual investment needed to restore Euro -
pean competitiveness at EUR 750-800bn. Two of the identified levers
are crucial. The first is R&D, an area where the gap with China has

widened significantly in recent years 4. The second is the deepening
of the Single Market, which is key to the economies of scale enjoyed
by Chinese corporate champions. Yet Europe does not lack resources,
not least its current account surplus (2.5% of GDP), which reflects a
continued abundance of savings. Several initiatives currently under
negotiation, including the Industrial Accelerator Act, are expected to
result in concrete measures (see table), even though their final scope

and implementation will take time.
THE EU’S TOOLBOX AGAINST CHINA
The European Union should not aim to rebalance its bilateral trade
balance with China, as it does reflect both competitive distortions and
greater R&D expenditures. The EU’s surplus with the rest of the wor -
ld demonstrates its continued competitiveness. Nevertheless, in the
short term, it must defend its market more firmly and in a targeted
manner. The meeting on October 8-9, as part of the Trade and Invest-

ment Consultation platform launched in late June, could yield welco -
me progress regarding rare-earth exports or access to the Chinese
market. However, the real challenge for Europe lies elsewhere. It must
transform itself from within by significantly increasing investment and
economies of scale, while diversifying its markets. These three levers
are essential for the EU to strengthen its sovereignty and enhance its
resilience to economic shocks.

Guillaume Derrien
guillaume.a.derrien@bnpparibas.com

盈餘 36.65 億元， YoY+17.43% ，預估稅後 EPS 26.30 元。  
三. 預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 28.80 元 
復盛 2027 年在高爾夫球業務穩定小幅成長 ， 加上民盛營運恢復成
長態勢，另外新業務事業開始小量出貨，有助復盛應用 2027 年營
運持續成長。宏遠預估復盛 2026 年營收金額 355.00 億元，
YoY+4.41% ，預估營業利益 56.03 億元， YoY+10.57% ，預估稅
後盈餘 40.14 億元， YoY+9.51% ，預估稅後 EPS 28.80 元。  
四. 投資建議買進，目標價 346 元 
觀察復盛應用歷史 PER 8~15X ， 2026 年 ROE 21.4% ，PER 
10.3X ，2027 年 ROE 19.4% ，PER 9.4X ，評價處在歷史低檔水
準 ， 加上復盛應用己切入水冷散熱零組件供應鏈 ， 有助評價提升，
故投資建議買進，目標價 346 元。 (12X 2027 年稅後 EPS)

2 
貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介  
復盛 (6670.TT) 成立於 1964 年，並 於1997 年掛牌上市 ， 復盛原有
三大事業部，分別為機械 (空壓機 )、運動器材 (高爾夫球 )與電子事
業部 (導線架 )。復盛 2008 年經勇德國際私募基金收購下市，將復
盛公司一分為三，將運動器材部門成立為復盛應用材料公司，機
械部門沿用復盛公司，電子事業部成立為復盛精密。復盛應用材
料資本額 13.94 億元，並於 2018 年掛牌上市。旗下生產據點有八
處，遍佈中國廣東、越南與台灣為主。客戶包括高爾夫球品牌廠，
如 Callaway 、TITLEIST 、DUNLOP、HONMA、TaylorMade 、PING、
PUMA、MIZUNO 、Honeywell 、YAMAHA 、SAFRAN 、SRIXON 、
Cleveland 等。

圖一、復盛公司沿革

資料來源：公司、宏遠投顧整理

二. 生產基地與產能概況  
復盛生產基地有八處，遍佈台灣、中國大陸、越南與泰國。

在台灣方面，有高雄與桃園三座工廠，其中高雄廠主要生產球棒，
桃園廠主要以高球頭的鈦合金前段製程、鈦合金鍛造製程為主，
嘉華盛位於高雄岡山，主要以航太引擎、油路系統產品為主。

中國大陸主要位於廣東中山，廣盛一廠主要生產碳纖產品與球棒
為主，廣盛二廠以高球頭鈦合金後段製程、鋼類球頭前後段製程
與鐵頭前後段製程為主，奧博主要以高爾夫球桿頭零配件為主。
越盛廠，主要以鋼類球頭前後段製程與鐵頭前後段製程為主。

美國 FSPT 廠，主要生產車用滑輪葉片與航太產品為主。民盛應
用生產基地位於台灣與泰國，主要生產滑冰靴、越野摩托車靴與
護具為主。

三. 2026 年營運概況及展望  
觀察高爾夫球桿頭全年市場量在 4,000 萬顆 (疫情前 )， 復盛年產能
在 1,600~1,700 萬顆計算，復盛市占率約 40% 左右，若以中高階
市場計算，市占率將超過 50% 。客戶包括高爾夫球品牌廠，如
Callaway 、Cleveland 、DUNLOP、HONMA、Honeywell 、MIZUNO 、
PING 、PUMA 、SAFRAN 、SRIXON 、TaylorMade 、TITLEIST 、
YAMAHA 等，前三大客戶營收比重在 50~55% 間。

復盛 01~08/2026 營收金額 215.85 億元， YoY+14.88% 。觀察美
國 2026 年前八月累計打球人次成長 1.1% 。上述顯示，美國高爾
夫球打球人次並未因為疫情結束而呈現衰退情況，高爾夫球業務
受惠新客戶在越南生產基地訂單開始放量，加上原有客戶新產品
持續貢獻，預估 2026 年高爾夫球產業營收金額 273.00 億元，
YoY+17.49%( 包括高球頭、組桿與高爾夫配件等 )；在非高爾夫球
產業在伯鑫 2026 年貢獻全年度營運，加上力野持續成長，預估
2026 年非高爾夫球產業營收金額 37.2 億元， YoY+6.93%( 航太、
球棒與其他 )；Minson 2026 年受到 EASTON 訂單減少拖累，預估
民盛 2026 年營收金額 29.8 億元， YoY-3.09% 。綜合上述，宏遠
預估復盛 2026 年合併營收金額 340 億元， YoY+14.13% 。

圖四、美國 2026 年前八月打球人次概況

資料來源：公司、宏遠投顧整理

預期 2027 年，高爾夫球業務在每年呈現微幅成長情況下，預估
2027 年高爾夫球產業營收金額 283.00 億元，YoY+3.66%( 包括高
球頭、組桿與高爾夫配件等 )；在非高爾夫球產業在航太、伯鑫與
新業務拓展帶動 ， 預估2027 年非高爾夫球產業營收金額 40 億元 ，
YoY+7.53%( 航太、球棒與其他 )；Minson 2027 年受惠 Alpinestar 
與 EASTON 兩大客戶成長帶動 ， 預估民盛2027 年營收金額 32.00
億元， YoY+7.38% 。綜合上述，宏遠預估復盛 2027 年合併營收
金額 355 億元， YoY+4.44% 。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

預估 2026 年稅後盈餘 36.65 億元 ，YoY+17.43% ，稅 後EPS 26.30
元

復盛 2026 年在高爾夫球業務受惠主力客戶訂單推升營運穩定 ， 加
上伯鑫貢獻全年度營運，另外業外無匯兌損失衝擊，整體獲利恢
復成長態勢。宏遠預估復盛 2026 年營收金額 340.00 億元，
YoY+14.13% ，預估營業利益 50.68 億元， YoY+9.4% ，預估稅後
盈餘 36.65 億元， YoY+17.43% ，預估稅後 EPS 26.30 元。

預估 2027 年稅後盈餘 40.14 億元， YoY+9.5% ，稅後 EPS 28.80
元

復盛 2027 年在高爾夫球業務穩定小幅成長 ， 加上民盛營運恢復成
長態勢，另外新業務事業開始小量出貨，有助復盛應用 2027 年營
運持續成長。宏遠預估復盛 2026 年營收金額 355.00 億元，
YoY+4.41% ，預估營業利益 56.03 億元， YoY+10.57% ，預估稅
後盈餘 40.14 億元， YoY+9.51% ，預估稅後 EPS 28.80 元。

推估 2026 年股利政策將配發現金股利 17.5 元 ， 現金殖利率6.5%

復盛配發現金股利為主，股利發放率 65~77% 。復盛 2025 年股利
政策，配發現金股利 16.00 元，股利發放率 71% 。宏遠推估復盛
2026 年股利政策，將配發現金股利 17.50 元，現金殖利率 6.5% 。

Document 519: 小康状態が続くルーマニアの通貨不安

Source PDF: all report pdf/小康状態が続くルーマニアの通貨不安.pdf
Report source: MURC
Date: 2026-09-15
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 9 月 15 日

MURC Focus

小康状態が続くルーマニアの通貨不安

～レウ相場の先行きを左右する政治不安と構造改革～

調査部 主任研究員 土田 陽介

1. 5 月の暴落以降は横ばいとなっているルーマニアの通貨 
ルーマニアの通貨レウ（複数形はレイ）の対ユーロ相場は、5 月に、イリエ＝ガブリル・ボロジャン首相が率いた
中道の連立政権が崩壊したことによって暴落した（図表 1）。具体的には、1 ユーロ＝5.10 レイ程度で横ばいであ
った為替レートが、5.25 レイ前後まで下落した。その後、レウの対ユーロ相場は横ばいで推移しており、通貨不安
は小康状態となっている。

この間、中銀であるルーマニア国立銀行（BNR）は、インフレが強含みであるにもかかわらず、政策金利を据え
置いている（図表 2）。こうしたことから、BNR による金融政策運営の面からレウ安に歯止めがかかったわけではな
いと考えられる。一方、ルーマニアの外貨準備は 4 月の 616 億ユーロから 7 月には 600 億ユーロまで減少して
おり、この間に BNR がレウ買いユーロ売りの為替介入を行った可能性を物語る（図表 3）。 
図表 1. レウの対ユーロ相場 図表 2. ルーマニアの物価と金利

（注）レウの複数形はレイ。  
（出所）ルーマニア国立銀行（BNR, Banca Națională a României）

〇ルーマニアの通貨レウの対ユーロ相場は、 5月の連立政権崩壊で暴落したが、 その後は横ばいで推移して
おり、通貨不安は小康状態となっている。 
〇ルーマニアでは政治不安が長期化しており、経常収支の赤字削減が進まないなど 、ファンダメンタルズに改
善が見られない。レウは依然として過大評価されており、下落余地が大きい。 
〇政権が構造改革の推進に向けた姿勢が堅持できなくなると投資家が判断すれば、レウ相場の下落につなが
る。政治不安の早期収束と構造改革の推進がレウ相場の先行きを大きく左右する。

### Page 2

8 月の外貨準備は 616 億ユーロと 7 月から回復している。これは欧州中銀（ECB）による最大 45 億ユーロの
レポラインを通じ、BNR が ECB よりユーロ資金を借り入れたためだと推察される。いずれにせよ、5 月の政権崩
壊に伴い暴落したレウの対ユーロ相場だが、直後から BNR が ECB の協力の下でレウ買いユーロ売り介入に努
めているため、レウの下落は免れているのだろう。

2. エネルギー危機と政治不安の中で経済の体質改善は遅れたまま 
通貨の安定には経済の基礎的条件（ファンダメンタルズ）の改善は不可欠であるものの、 BNR は政策金利の
引き上げに慎重である。一方、2月に生じたイラン発のエネルギーショックに伴いインフレが強含みであるため、実
質金利は依然としてマイナス圏にある。加えて、長期化する政治不安のため政府による財政引き締めが一向に進
まず、名目 GDP（国内総生産）の 8%程度の財政赤字が常態化している。

ボロジャン首相が率いる連立政権が崩壊した後も、同首相は暫定政権を率いている。この間、ニクショル・ダン
大統領はエウゲン・トマク欧州議会議員やブラショフ県議会のアドリアン・ヴェシュテア議長を新たな首相候補に
任命したが、いずれも不成功に終わった。6 月中下旬にはボロジャン首相が党首を務める中道の自由主義政党
である国民自由党が分裂する事態となり、政治不安に拍車がかかった。

こうしたことから、ルーマニアの経常収支は名目 GDP の 8%程度の赤字が定着しており、改善が見られない
（図表4）。こうした環境の下では、ファンダメンタルズの観点からレウ相場の安定は見込めないため、BNR とECB
によるレポラインを通じたレウ買い介入以外に、ルーマニアの通貨不安を和らげる術はない。ただしこれはあくま
で対症療法であるため、事態の抜本的な改善にはつながらない。

図表 3. ルーマニアの外貨準備 図表 4. ルーマニアの経常収支

### Page 3

3. 政治不安と改革後退がさらなる通貨暴落を招く恐れ 
ルーマニアレウの対ユーロ実質レートは、コロナショック前後から名目レートとの乖離が顕著となっており、10 年
前に比べると 10%程度、増価している（図表 5）。一方、名目レートは 15%ほど減価しているため、この間の実質
レートの増価の主因は高インフレとなる。この高インフレは、ルーマニア経済のファンダメンタルズの悪化に伴うも
のであるから、ルーマニアレウの価値は過大に評価されており、修正＝下落余地が大きいと考えられる。

したがって、何らかのリスク要因が強く意識されると、ルーマニアの通貨は暴落を余儀なくされる公算が大きい。
足元では、政治不安の高まりが最大のリスク要因となる。依然として組閣ができないルーマニアであるが、この状
態が長期化すれば、ルーマニアが EU との間で約束している構造改革が後退し、EU からの支援が縮小ないし
は撤回される可能性が意識される。

現在、ダン大統領のもとで、ルーマニアは EU との間で約束している 6 つの主要改革法の公布を急いでいる。
6 つのうち、すでに 4 つの法律（国土計画・都市計画・建設に関する法、冷暖房部門の脱炭素化に関する法、国
税庁（ANAF）・税関職員の給与改革法、公務員キャリア制度改革法）は公布済みだが、残り 2 法（公職者の資産
申告や利益相反に関する規則を見直す法、公務員給与法）に関しては公務員の反発が強い。

こうした構造改革は、EU 復興基金からの資金配分を受けるための必須条件（コンディショナリティー）となって
いる。法が全て公布されても、政治不安の長期化で、ルーマニアの政権が構造改革の推進に向けた姿勢が堅持
できなくなると投資家が判断すれば、EU や ECB からのサポートが後退する可能性が意識され、レウ相場の下落
につながると懸念される。政治不安の早期収束と構造改革の推進がレウ相場の先行きを大きく左右する。

図表 5. ルーマニアレウの対ユーロ実質レート 図表 6. 主要改革 6 法の概要

141.45% ，成为当⽇最耀眼的 “ 吸⾦王 ” ；这⼀成交额不仅位居榜⾸，且远超第⼆名华夏上证科创
板 50ETF 的 89.37 亿元，资⾦在⼤盘蓝筹⽅向的集中涌⼊态势⼀⽬了然。

14 ⽇的 14.83 亿元增⻓ 66.25% 。

0.97% ，随后成交量急剧放⼤，盘中出现 441 万元、 221 万元等⼤额买单，价格迅速回升⾄ 4.236
元附近，跌幅收窄⾄ 0.14% 。华夏上证 50ETF ⾛势如出⼀辙， 14:40 价格触及 2.845 元、下跌
1.04% 后，⼤单涌⼊推动成交额快速攀升，最终收盘仅跌 0.31% 。两只 ETF 的尾盘放量，均发⽣

在市场情绪最为悲观的时刻，护盘意图不⾔⾃明。
9 ⽉底以来超 1400 亿元资⾦涌⼊，宽基 ETF 成 “ 避⻛港 ”
在 10 ⽉ 15 ⽇成交放量后，易⽅达沪深 300ETF 单⽇净流⼊超 188 亿元，易⽅达创业板 ETF 净流⼊
55.3 亿元，华夏科创 50ETF 净流⼊ 65.69 亿元。
拉⻓周期来看，⾃ 9 ⽉ 28 ⽇以来，股票 ETF 合计净流⼊ 1407 亿元，其中，有 1168 亿元净流⼊宽
基 ETF 。
这⼀趋势在 10 ⽉以来呈现加速态势，以 10 ⽉ 8 ⽇ —15 ⽇ 6 个交易⽇来看，股票 ETF 整体资⾦净流
⼊超 1040 亿元。其中，跟踪沪深 300 指数、科创 50 指数、创业板指等宽基 ETF 净流⼊明显。从近
5 ⽇⻆度观测，近期资⾦流⼊跟踪沪深 300 指数的 ETF 超 272 亿元，跟踪科创 50 指数的 ETF 超 169
亿元。

2 条评论
近期全球权益市场估值受到压制。美联储加息叠加 AI ⽅向叙事受到外围光模块消息扰动， 9 ⽉底
以来， A 股市场波动有所加⼤。多家基⾦公司认为，当前市场有利因素正在积累。
“ 向后看，前期拥挤度带来的筹码压⼒消化接近尾声，情绪指标进⼀步回探，已接近中期极低
点，伴随新⼀轮稳增⻓政策窗⼝徐徐展开，市场有望获得⽀撑，不宜再过度悲观。 ” 嘉实基⾦表
⽰。
⾸先，在政策⽅⾯，节前以地产按揭贴息为核⼼的新⼀轮稳增⻓政策落地，印证新的稳增⻓窗
⼝开启，期待政策继续发⼒。其次，情绪指标⽅⾯，杠杆资⾦持续退潮， 9 ⽉融资流出 500 多亿
元，截⾄ 10 ⽉ 13 ⽇，两融余额已跌破 2.55 万亿元，回到今年 4 ⽉初的⽔平。 10 ⽉ 14 ⽇，市场成

交量缩⾄ 1.42 万亿元，为 2025 年 7 ⽉以来最低⽔平。因此，从历史来看，缩量到极限后，往往不
宜再悲观以对。
此外，对于科技成⻓板块的回调，嘉实基⾦表⽰，是美国联邦通信委员会（ FCC ）消息扰动和
外围科技股波动共振影响，但并⾮产业趋势的逆转。伴随持续回调后 “ 估值 + 拥挤度 ” 消化，业绩
驱动与⾃主可控有望形成双重催化。因此，回撤后逢低布局的⻓期胜率往往优于市场喧嚣时追
⾼。

### Page 1

欧 州 経 済 展 望
2026年9月
調査部 マクロ経済研究センター

### Page 2

◆４～６月期の成長率は上方改定
足元のユーロ圏景気は底堅く推移 。４～６月期
の実質ＧＤＰ成長率は前期比年率＋ 2.6％と、
速報値（同＋1.8％）から上方修正 。ただし、多
国籍企業による事業活動の影響で変動が激しいア
イルランドの上方改定が主因。同国を除いた成長率
は潜在成長率並みのペース。
需要項目別では 、在庫変動や総固定資本形成
の減少が成長率を下押しした一方、個人消費や輸
出の増加が全体を押し上げ。
◆物価高と利上げで景気は先行き減速へ
先行きの景気は、インフレの加速やＥＣＢの利上
げを受けて減速する見通し 。８月の消費者物価は
前年比＋3.2％と、前月から伸びが拡大。既往のエ
ネルギーコストの増大がコア品目にも今後波及するこ
とで、インフレ率は年末にかけて上昇が続くと予想。
インフレ率の上昇が見込まれる なか、ＥＣＢは９
月の理事会で政策金利を0.25％ポイント引き上げ。
金利の上昇が 、金利感応度の高い住宅投資や設

備投資などを下押しする公算。
当面のリスクは、原油・天然ガス価格のさらなる上
昇。為替面や賃金面からのインフレ圧力は今のとこ
ろ落ち着いているものの、資源高が長引けば、ユーロ
の減価や賃上げ要求の強まりを通じて、基調的なイ
ンフレ圧力が高まる可能性も 。その場合、ＥＣＢが
追加利上げを行うことで、景気が下振れる恐れ。

◆ＡＩ需要が生産活動を押し上げ

長率は前月比+0.4%と、高めの伸び。建設業と鉱
工業が増産に転じたほか、サービス業の伸びが加速。
業種別にみると 、旺盛なＡＩ需要を背景に 、ＩＴ
関連の情報サービス業の 事業活動が活発化した ほ
か、コンピュータ ・電子製品・光学機械の生産が拡
大。
◆足元の設備投資は好調
４～６月期の実質総固定資本形成は前期比年
率＋5.0%と、堅調な伸び。情報通信技術（ＩＣ
Ｔ）関連の機械投資が大幅に増えたことが主因。
先行き、設備投資は底堅く推移する見通し。資材
価格の高騰や長期金利の上昇が重石となる一方、
世界的なＡＩ需要の高まりがＩＣＴ関連の投資
を今後も押し上げると予想。
◆個人消費は減速する見通し
４～６月期の実質個人消費は前期比年率＋
1.1%と、底堅く推移。内訳をみると、娯楽サービス
などを中心に幅広い品目の消費が増加。
もっとも、先行きはインフレ加速を背景に 、個人消

費 は 減 速 す る 見 通 し。 電力 ・ ガ ス 市 場 局
（Ofgem）は、足元の資源高を踏まえて 、2027
年１～３月期にかけて 、エネルギー価格の上限を
引き上げる見込み。

### Page 5

欧州
(株)日本総合研究所 欧州経済展望 2026年９月
◆足元にかけて資源高が加速
欧州では、原油・天然ガスの先物価格が高騰。北
海ブレント原油価格が100ドル台/バレルに上昇して
いるほか、天然ガスの代表的指標であるオランダＴ
ＴＦは一時80ユーロ台/MWhと、本年２月末のイ
ラン軍事衝突直後を上回る水準まで急騰。
原油高の主因は、中東情勢の緊迫化。イエメンの
親イラン武装組織フーシが 、ホルムズ海峡を迂回す
るルートとして機能してきたバブ・エル・マンデブ海峡へ
の影響力を強めたほか、サウジアラビア国内のパイプ
ラインが攻撃されたことで 、同国産石油の供給が下
振れるとの懸念が増大。
ガス高の主因は、欧州における需給ひっ迫 。足元
の天然ガス在庫率は、過去５年間の同時期に比べ
て最も低い水準。ホルムズ海峡の封鎖によってカター
ル産ガスの輸入が減少したほか、夏場に生じた熱波
によってガス火力発電の需要が増加したことが背景 。
◆原油・ガス高は南欧経済を中心に打撃が大
資源価格がさらに上昇する場合 、インフレ率の上

昇やＥＣＢの追加利上げを通じて 、ユーロ圏全体
の景気が下振れる可能性。
国別では、石油・天然ガスへの依存度が高いイタリ
アやスペインへの打撃が大きくなる公算。資源高によ
る企業のコスト増や家計の購買力低下が 、内需を
下押しする可能性 。こうした南欧諸国は 、これまで
ユーロ圏景気をけん引してきただけに 、資源高を起
点として景気が落ち込むリスクに注意。

### Page 6

欧州
(株)日本総合研究所 欧州経済展望 2026年９月5
欧州経済見通し：ユーロ圏・英国ともに当面は減速
欧州各国の経済・物価見通し
【ユーロ相場見通し】
◆当面、ユーロは弱含む見通し
８月のユーロドル相場は 、中旬にかけて 1.17ドル台へ
ユーロ高 ・ドル安が進行 。米雇用統計が市場予想を下
回ったことなどから、ＦＲＢによる利上げ観測が後退したこ
とが背景。その後、８月末にかけて1.16ドル台半ばを中心
に横ばい圏で推移。米物価指標が市場予想を上回った一
方、ユーロ圏の景況感改善がユーロの下支えに 。９月入り
後は、下旬にかけて1.13ドル台後半へユーロ安・ドル高が
進行。中東情勢の緊迫化に伴う原油高を背景に 、投資
家によるリスク回避のドル買い圧力が高まったほか、ＦＲＢ
による利上げがドルを押し上げ。
当面のユーロドル相場は、ユーロ安基調で推移する見込
み。ＥＣＢが政策金利を据え置く一方、ＦＲＢが追加利
上げを行い、欧米の金利差が拡大することが背景。
＜ユーロ圏＞
◆年末にかけて減速、来年から持ち直し

ユーロ圏の景気は、年末にかけて減速する見通し 。
中東危機による物価や金利の上昇が内需を下押し
する見込み。ただし、南欧諸国を中心とする良好な
雇用環境が個人消費を下支えするほか、ドイツの政
府支出や、復興基金を原資とする各国の公共投資
が増加することで、景気の底割れは回避。インフレ圧
力の緩和を受けて、来年以降、景気は持ち直しへ。
先行きのリスクは 、中東危機の長期化。原油・天
然ガス価格が高止まりする場合 、インフレが想定以
上に長引くことで 、石油・ガスへのエネルギー依存度
が高い南欧諸国を中心に個人消費が大きく落ち込
む恐れ。ＥＣＢが複数回の利上げを行い、ユーロ圏
全体の内需が一段と下押しされる可能性も。
＜英国＞
◆家計部門を中心に、景気は減速へ
英国の景気は、減速する見通し 。世界的なＡＩ
需要の高まりが企業の生産活動や設備投資を下
支えする一方 、物価高や雇用情勢の悪化が個人
消費の重石となる公算。
先行きのリスクは、ユーロ圏と同様、中東危機の長
期化。資源価格が高止まりすれば、景気が大きく下

振れる恐れも。

欧州金利見通し：ＥＣＢ、ＢＯＥともに様子見姿勢へ
＜ユーロ圏＞
◆ＥＣＢ：当面は様子見姿勢を維持
ＥＣＢは、９月の理事会で政策金利 を0.25％
ポイント引き上げ。既往の利上げによる物価抑制効
果を見極めるため 、当面は様子見姿勢を続ける見
通し。
中東危機が長期化するリスクシナリオでは、インフレ
率が一段と上昇することで、複数回の追加利上げを
行う可能性も。
独10年債利回りは 、インフレ懸念や財政拡張を
背景に、上昇基調が続く見通し。
＜英国＞
◆ＢＯＥ：当面様子見、利下げ再開は来年
ＢＯＥは、９月の金融政策委員会 で政策金利
の据え置きを決定。今後、エネルギー価格の上昇を
主因にインフレ加速が見込まれるものの 、個人消費
の低迷を背景に、需要面からのインフレ圧力は弱く、
ＢＯＥは様子見姿勢を維持すると予想。
リスクシナリオでは、ＥＣＢと同様に、利上げを迫ら
れる可能性も。
英10年債利回りは、インフレ懸念が上昇圧力とな
る一方、景気の減速が低下圧力となることで 、横ば

い圏で推移する見通し 。ただし、バーナム政権が財
政拡張を進める場合、財政リスクプレミアムの拡大を
通じて長期金利が急騰する恐れも。

AI輸出規制の実効性と限界 ―アメリカ対中国を事例とする技術管理政策への示唆 ― 福田直之 9月10日
FIMAレポ活用で為替介入余地は拡大も、円相場への影響は一時的 ―円高トレンドへの
転換には、日米金利差などの円安要因の変化が必要 ― 西村朱央 9月4日

### Page 1

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
